# TODO — Qwen 3.5/3.6 Native Metal Port

**Goal:** Native Metal inference for Qwen 3.5 9B and 3.6 27B (arch=qwen35), targeting ≥10% faster than llama.cpp HEAD on decode, with cosine≈1.0 correctness.

**Target model:** `Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf` (primary debug target) → scale to `Qwen3.6-27B-Q4_K_M.gguf`.

**llama.cpp baseline source:** `~/SrcArchives/AI/llama.cpp` (HEAD).

**Protocol:** v13.2. Quadrumvirate on each phase start. LOGBOOK in conversation, persistent state in LANDMARKS.md.

---

## Phase 0 — Verification & infrastructure

- [x] **0.1** Create LANDMARKS.md + TODO.md
- [x] **0.2** Survey llama.cpp HEAD for: qwen35 arch implementation, DeltaNet (not Mamba), M-RoPE, Q4_K Metal kernels
- [x] **0.3** llama.cpp HEAD baseline: 9B Q4_K_M pp64=458, tg64=43.5 tok/s on M2 Max (FA=0)
- [x] **0.4** qwen35 hparams parsing: `get_int_array` helper + `Qwen35Hparams` struct; 5 spec examples passing

## Phase 1a — Q4_K CPU path

- [x] **1a.1** `Dequant.dequantize_q4_k` in `src/ml/gguf/dequant.cr` (block: d/dmin/scales[12]/qs[128] = 144 B)
- [x] **1a.2** `QuantMatmul.matmul_add_q4k` in `src/ml/gguf/quant_matmul.cr`
- [x] **1a.3** Correctness vs libggml: dequant bit-identical (cos=1.0, max_diff=0) on ssm_alpha/ffn_up; fused matmul cos≈1.0, max_diff≈2.4e-7 on 4096×4096

## Phase 1b — CPU-reference Qwen 3.5 9B forward

- [ ] **1b.1** `CausalDecoder` extensions for qwen35: partial M-RoPE with sections, GQA 4:1 broadcast, Mamba SSM layer, full-attn every 4 layers, SwiGLU FFN, RMSNorm
- [ ] **1b.2** LM head with Q4_K
- [ ] **1b.3** Correctness: cosine≈1.0 vs llama.cpp across all 32 layers on 1 token

## Phase 2 — Metal Q4_K kernels

- [ ] **2.1** `dequantize_q4_K_fn` in Metal (Q5_K pattern minus qh)
- [ ] **2.2** `simd_gemm_q4k` + `simd_gemm_q4k_moe` in `gemm_simd.metal`
- [ ] **2.3** `simd_mm_q4k` + `simd_mm_q4k_moe` + `batched_mm_q4k` in `gemm_mm.metal`
- [ ] **2.4** Correctness: cosine≈1.0 vs CPU Q4_K, speedup vs reference

## Phase 3a — Metal attention: head_dim=256 + GQA

- [ ] **3a.1** Read llama.cpp M-RoPE implementation — document exact formula (**BLOCKER for 3a**)
- [ ] **3a.2** Rewrite `attention_causal.metal`: dynamic head_dim (no fixed `float4 qr[16]`), kv-head broadcast
- [ ] **3a.3** Rewrite `attention_flash.metal`: same + flash version
- [ ] **3a.4** Partial M-RoPE fused in QKV
- [ ] **3a.5** Correctness: full-attn layer 9B cosine≈1.0, no regression on nomic BERT

## Phase 3b — Metal Mamba

- [ ] **3b.1** SoTA harvest: Mamba-2 paper, llama.cpp ggml-cuda/mamba.cu, reference selective_scan
- [ ] **3b.2** `selective_scan_fwd` Metal kernel
- [ ] **3b.3** SSM state management in BufferPool
- [ ] **3b.4** Correctness: SSM layer 9B cosine≈1.0

## Phase 3.5 — State save/load

- [x] **3.5.1** Serialize: KV-buffers (full-attn layers) + DeltaNet recurrent state (conv + SSM) + metadata
- [x] **3.5.2** Deserialize / restore
- [x] **3.5.3** Prompt KV cache: exact lookup by session/hash, artifact restore, fail-closed artifact hash/model checks
- [x] **3.5.4** pg_sorted_heap metadata adapter for prompt KV cache (external artifact path + sorted session/hash index)
- [x] **3.5.5** Correctness: exact top1 + full-logit close restore on 9B
- [x] **3.5.6** Longest-prefix prompt cache: token-hash prefix lookup + exact suffix replay

## Phase 4 — Optimization: beat llama.cpp

- [ ] **4.1** Fused RMSNorm + partial M-RoPE + QKV projection
- [ ] **4.2** Fused SwiGLU
- [ ] **4.3** Weight-tile cache in threadgroup memory
- [ ] **4.4** ComputeGraph wave scheduling for decoder
- [ ] **4.5** Benchmark: ≥10% faster than llama.cpp HEAD on 9B decode
- [x] **4.6** Exact first-run prefill shortcut: skip output RMSNorm/lm-head for non-final prompt tokens
- [x] **4.7** Port llama.cpp-style chunked fused DeltaNet scan primitive
- [x] **4.8** Port chunked recurrent prep primitives: conv-shift, alpha/beta, Q/K L2 norm
- [ ] **4.9** True layerwise/microbatch prefill for known prompt tokens
  - [x] Experimental CPU-orchestrated layerwise recurrent chunk path (`QWEN35_PREFILL_CHUNK=1`) with correctness gate
  - [x] Falsifier: CPU-orchestrated chunking is slower than current whole-token Metal decode wave; keep default off
  - [x] GPU-resident recurrent layer chunk primitive; default-on prefill chunking improves pp64 p50 from `52.87 tok/s` to `56.70 tok/s`
  - [x] Reuse Q4_K simdgroup-matrix GEMM inside GPU-resident recurrent prefill chunks; pp64 p50 improves from `56.70 tok/s` to `70.38 tok/s`
  - [x] Chunk full-attention prefill layers on Metal; pp64 p50 improves from `70.38 tok/s` to `143.18 tok/s`
  - [x] Enable Q5_K/Q6_K batch GEMM in prefill chunks with GPU-side F32↔F16 conversion; pp64 p50 improves from `143.18 tok/s` to `278.50 tok/s`
  - [x] Keep DeltaNet chunk state rows register-resident across the token scan; pp64 p50 improves from `278.50 tok/s` to `308.86 tok/s`
  - [x] Keep consecutive recurrent prefill layers GPU-resident; pp64 p50 improves from `308.86 tok/s` to `327.54 tok/s`
  - [x] Batch the final prompt token into prefill and run only fused lm-head top1; pp64 p50 improves from `308.82 tok/s` to `358.44 tok/s`
  - [x] Fast-path full Q4_K GEMM output tiles with direct simdgroup stores; pp64 p50 improves from `358.44 tok/s` to `373.80 tok/s`
  - [x] Fuse full-attention chunk boundaries into following recurrent runs; total pp64 syncs drop from `17` to `10`, warm profile improves `177.20 ms` → `172.53 ms` (small/noisy but positive paired A/B)
  - [x] Add prefill attribution harness and matmul shape counters; pp64 shows FFN up/gate Q4_K dominates logical weight traffic (`1728 MiB`), Q6/Q4 FFN-down follows (`1062 MiB` combined), and Q5/Q6 batch GEMM is mandatory (`~170 ms` default vs `~367 ms` with `QWEN35_Q56K_BATCH_GEMM_OFF=1`)
  - [x] Add `--prepare-state` to the prefill attribution harness so kernel/profile work can measure prepared-state prompt ingest separately from lazy first-touch state allocation; pp64 prepared profile measured `142.31 ms` p50 / `449.72 tok/s` with the same hot-shape mix.
  - [x] In-place SwiGLU staging for chunked prefill FFN paths; exact aliasing path passes specs and small pp64 A/B improves default by `~0.48 ms` vs `QWEN35_SWIGLU_INPLACE_OFF=1`
  - [x] Falsifier: dual Q4_K gate/up+SwiGLU GEMM reduced dispatch count but was slower at pp64 (`174.78 ms` default vs `168.65 ms` off), likely from register pressure and extra barriers
  - [x] Specialize final full-attention prefill layer for last-row logits; final layer still writes all K/V but computes attention+FFN only for the final row, improving pp64 A/B from `173.86 ms` off to `170.00 ms` default
  - [x] Falsifier: direct SwiGLU-to-F16 activation staging for Q6 FFN-down was exact in focused specs but slower at pp64 (`170.02 ms` default vs `167.52 ms` off), so activation staging is not the current wall
  - [x] Falsifier: double-buffered Q6_K GEMM compiled and passed focused forward specs, but pp64 A/B was neutral (`168.22 ms` default vs `168.25 ms` off; p50 slightly worse), so Q6 barriers are not the next leverage point
  - [x] Raise default prefill chunk size from `64` to `1024`; pp256 A/B improves from `711.92 ms` p50 at chunk 64 to `522.76 ms` p50 default, while pp64 is unchanged within noise
  - [x] Batch the final long-prompt suffix instead of falling back to single-token final decode when prompt exceeds chunk size; pp2048 A/B improves from `4987.29 ms` p50 off to `4750.20 ms` default
  - [x] Raise default prefill chunk size again from `1024` to `2048`; pp2048 p50 improves from `4755.48 ms` at chunk 1024 to `4578.57 ms` at chunk 2048, while pp1024 is unchanged within noise
  - [x] Raise default prefill chunk size from `2048` to `4096` for long prompts; pp4096 one-shot p50 improves from `11862.78 ms` at chunk 2048 to `11524.63 ms` at chunk 4096
  - [x] Make default prefill chunk size memory-aware; 64GB M2 Max selects `8192` and pp8192 one-shot A/B improves from `35219.27 ms` at chunk 4096 to `33981.42 ms` default, while smaller systems fall back to `4096`/`2048`
  - [x] Add grouped command-buffer attribution for fused prefill waves; pp256 shows all `full+rec` groups are nearly flat (`~61.2-61.7 ms` wait) and `rec0-2` is smaller (`~46.4 ms`), so there is no single pathological layer group to attack
  - [x] Add scoped matmul attribution for prefill/decode phases; pp256 confirms `prefill.rec.ffn_upgate` dominates logical weight traffic (`1296 MiB`), followed by recurrent Q5 projection (`528 MiB`) and recurrent FFN-down (`796.5 MiB` combined Q6/Q4); decode prompt64/gen4 shows the same recurrent FFN/upgate and projection dominance (`5184 MiB` and `2112 MiB`)
  - [x] Harden prefill A/B attribution: `--compare-env` now runs paired interleaved trials and reports pair wins, avoiding block-order drift false positives
  - [x] Falsifier: lowering the GEMM route threshold so b8 prefill uses Q4/Q5/Q6 GEMM switches the routes correctly but slows pp8 (`140.70 ms` p50 default GEMV-at-b8 vs `158.74 ms` p50 GEMM-at-b8); keep `GEMM_BATCH_THRESHOLD=8`
  - [x] Falsifier: fusing final full-attn-last and lm-head top1 into one command buffer reduced pp64 syncs (`10` → `9`) but slowed wall reps in sequential release checks (`162.32 ms` p50 off vs `165.67 ms` p50 fused), so the removed sync is not worth the larger final command buffer
  - [x] Falsifier: a 256-thread Q4_K batch-64 GEMM reduced nominal batch tiles but did not survive benchmark adversary; same-process attribution was slightly positive (`164.51 ms` default vs `165.41 ms` off), but matched llama benchmark regressed (`378.72 tok/s` b64 vs `394.94 tok/s` current), so the branch was removed
  - [x] Falsifier: direct Q56_K FFN-down half-output add avoided an explicit `f16_to_f32` buffer but was neutral at pp64 (`167.22 ms` default vs `167.30 ms` branch p50, wins `4/8`), so conversion removal is not enough leverage
  - [x] Falsifier: llama.cpp-style single-buffer Q4_K GEMM with Q4-only `6144` byte threadgroup memory passed focused specs and improved standalone `4096x12288 b64` op attribution (`3.171 ms` → `2.975 ms`), but pp64 wall regressed (`167.39 ms` default p50 vs `176.01 ms` branch p50), so it is not a default prefill win
  - [x] Route tiny Q4_K prefill projections (`out_dim <= 64`, especially recurrent alpha/beta) through GEMV instead of underfilled 64-row GEMM tiles; pp64 paired A/B improves default from `164.04 ms` off to `156.14 ms` on (`8/8` wins), and matched llama comparison moves native prefill to `408.02 tok/s` p50 vs llama `463.3 tok/s`
  - [x] Falsifier: extending the tiny-Q4 GEMV rule to `out_dim <= 1024` makes pp64 slower (`165.86 ms` p50 at threshold 64 vs `169.08 ms` at threshold 1024, wins `8/8` for threshold 64), so keep the rule narrow
  - [x] Falsifier: routing small Q5/Q6 prefill projections (`out_dim <= 1024`) through GEMV instead of batch GEMM is also slower at pp64 (`165.07 ms` default vs `167.05 ms` GEMV, wins `8/8` for default)
  - [x] Disable Crystal GC during multi-token prefill hot path with `QWEN35_PREFILL_GC_GUARD_OFF=1` escape hatch; pp64 paired A/B improves default from `158.00 ms` off to `151.80 ms` on (`8/8` wins), and matched llama comparison moves native prefill to `421.41 tok/s` p50 vs llama `463.04 tok/s`
  - [x] Falsifier: direct Q5_K F32-input/F32-output batch GEMM compiled and passed focused specs, but pp64 paired A/B was neutral/negative (`152.56 ms` default vs `152.52 ms` branch p50, avg worse and wins `3/8`), so Q5 conversion removal is not enough leverage
  - [x] Falsifier: using fast command buffers for prefill helper/group paths compiled and passed specs, but pp64 was neutral (`152.02 ms` default vs `151.97 ms` fast, wins `2/8`) and pp256 slightly worse (`490.49 ms` default vs `490.79 ms` fast), so command-buffer creation is not the remaining gap
  - [x] Remove avoidable host churn in Qwen35 prefill boundaries and Q5/Q6 GEMM staging: shared-buffer reads no longer zero-fill before copy, Q56 zero bias buffers are cleared directly once, and Q56 F16 conversion scratch is reused by size; focused specs pass and pp64 smoke remains around `151.80 ms` p50
  - [x] Falsifier: adding a Q5/Q6 GEMM `no-bias` mode (`apply_gelu=2`) compiled and passed focused specs, but pp64 paired A/B was neutral/negative (`151.84 ms` default vs `151.80 ms` bias path off, wins `3/8`), so the bias add/read is not material enough to justify a kernel mode
  - [x] Add exact Q4_K half-input prefill GEMM (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_GEMM_OFF=1` disables it): Q4 prefill matmuls preconvert F32 activations to F16 once per matmul instead of per output tile; pp64 paired A/B improves default by `~1.28 ms` (`8/8` wins), pp256 improves by `~5.06 ms` (`6/6` wins), and matched pp64 moves native prefill to `424.96 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `462.9 tok/s`
  - [x] Falsifier: fully pre-dequantized/F16-expanded Q4 FFN weights are not the next prefill lever. A synthetic `4096x12288` F16 GEMM lower-bound was slower than the current compressed Q4_H16 route at b64/b128/b256 (`1.927/3.731/6.872 ms` F16 p50 vs `1.401/2.413/3.132 ms` Q4_H16 p50), so expanding hot Q4 weights to F16 increases traffic more than it removes dequant work.
  - [x] Add exact Q5_K recurrent-qkv half-output prefill path (`QWEN35_Q5_QKV_H16_CONV_OFF=1` disables it): Q5 qkv GEMM output stays F16 and the recurrent conv-shift chunk reads half directly; pp64 paired A/B improves default by `~0.28 ms` (`6/8` wins), pp256 by `~0.52 ms` (`5/6` wins)
  - [x] Reuse Q4_K half-input conversion across paired FFN gate/up matmuls for large prefill batches (`QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_GEMM_OFF=1` disables it, active at batch `>=256`): pp256 isolated paired A/B improves default by `~1.56 ms` avg / `~2.21 ms` p50 (`7/10` wins), pp512 opt-in A/B improved by `~1.82 ms` (`4/4` pair wins), and pp64 is gated off after adversary A/B showed the pair path is not reliable at short prompts
  - [x] Falsifier: fusing only the `gate/up -> SwiGLU` activation stage is too small to explain the prefill gap. A wait-only `4096x12288` Q4-pair microbench measured `pair_plus_swiglu` almost equal to the pair GEMMs alone at b64/b128/b256 (`1.877/3.268/6.119 ms` vs `1.861/3.255/6.063 ms`), so the remaining WBA lever must fuse a larger FFN diamond or improve the Q4/Q6 matmul kernels themselves.
  - [x] Historical falsifier, later refreshed: an earlier temporary branch sharing one H16 activation conversion between recurrent Q5 qkv and Q4 gate/z projections compiled and passed focused specs, but pp256 paired A/B was flat (`485.56 ms` default vs `485.59 ms` opt-in, wins `4/8`) with the then-current routes. Later kernel/routing changes made the same exact cleanup small-positive enough to retain as `QWEN35_REC_PROJ_SHARED_H16_OFF=1`; treat this old line as stale evidence, not an active refutation.
  - [x] Add conversion-kernel attribution to the prefill profile report so future quiet runs can separate duplicated `F32<->F16` activation/output traffic from quantized weight traffic; smoke pp16 report shows per-row percentages, matmul/conversion logical traffic totals, and mix (`97.50%` matmul / `2.50%` conversion)
  - [x] Add matched benchmark support for exact prompt-cache restore mode (`--native-prefill-cache`): after one seeded prefill, restoring the saved `.qkv` state measures pp64 p50 `52.28 ms` / `1224.28 tok/s` vs llama.cpp `432.72 tok/s` (`+182.93%`) under the same harness; this is a repeated/session prompt-cache speedup, not a first-run prefill replacement
  - [x] Add explicit Metal state-buffer preparation (`Qwen35CPU.prepare_state_metal!` and `--native-prefill-prepare-state`) so server-style benchmarks can exclude first-touch KV/DeltaNet buffer allocation from the timed prefill. pp64 fresh-state prepared mode measured `142.31 ms` / `449.73 tok/s` vs llama.cpp `464.78 tok/s` (`-3.24%`), while default first-run in the same binary measured `149.99 ms` / `426.70 tok/s`.
  - [x] Harden benchmark quiet-mode checks with both per-process and aggregate CPU thresholds (`--load-warning-threshold`, `--load-total-warning-threshold`) so noisy multi-process desktops do not silently pass `--require-quiet`; aggregate warnings now print top CPU contributors
  - [x] Add `--wait-quiet-ms` / `--quiet-poll-ms` to Qwen35 benchmark harnesses so queued A/B runs can wait for a quiet host before measuring instead of immediately aborting or recording noisy timings
  - [x] Guarded baseline refresh: with `--require-quiet --wait-quiet-ms=60000`, pp64 attribution reports p50 `151.73 ms` / `421.80 tok/s`, traffic mix `90.69%` matmul / `9.31%` conversion; matched llama.cpp prompt64/gen64 reports native prefill `426.01 tok/s` p50 vs llama `461.90 tok/s` (`-7.77%`) and native decode `47.60 tok/s` p50 vs llama `45.35 tok/s` (`+4.96%`)
  - [x] Falsifier: removing the inner `simdgroup_barrier(mem_none)` calls from the Q4_K H16 prefill GEMM passed focused Qwen specs (`14 examples, 0 failures`) but regressed guarded pp64 attribution from baseline p50 `151.73 ms` to `154.49 ms`, so keep the barriers
  - [x] Add a guarded paired decode A/B harness for env-tuned wave experiments; guarded trials confirm `QWEN35_WAVE_CHUNK_LAYERS=2` beats unchunked `0` by `~1.34 ms/tok` (`6/6` wins), while `2` vs `4` and `1` vs `2` remain neutral/noisy, so keep default `2`
  - [x] Falsifier: writing Q4 FFN pair input as H16 directly from add+RMSNorm was exact in focused specs, but pp256 paired A/B was neutral (`506.53 ms` default vs `506.29 ms` opt-in, wins `5/10`), so the separate conversion kernel is not the current wall
  - [x] Relaxed-load baseline after CPU-core clarification: sequential pp64 attribution remains stable at p50 `150.80 ms` / `424.39 tok/s`, pp256 at p50 `486.56 ms` / `526.14 tok/s`; matched pp64/gen64 reports native prefill `422.41 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `461.35 tok/s` (`-8.44%`) and native decode `46.59 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `44.38 tok/s` (`+4.98%`)
  - [x] Falsifier: nearby decode scheduler/glue knobs do not recover the missing margin: `QWEN35_WAVE_CHUNK_LAYERS=3` is neutral vs default `2` (`4/8`, delta `+0.013 ms/tok`), `QWEN35_WAVE_FAST_CMD=1` is neutral vs off (`3/6`, delta `-0.018 ms/tok`), `QWEN35_REC_CONVSHIFT_FUSED=1` is slightly worse/noisy (`3/8`, delta `+0.025 ms/tok`), and `QWEN35_DN_POST_FUSED=1` is neutral (`3/8`, delta `-0.014 ms/tok`)
  - [x] Falsifier: matching llama.cpp's Q6_K GEMV `NR0=2` passed focused specs but slowed decode sync profile (`25.67 ms/tok` vs `24.60 ms/tok` old binary in same sequence), so keep local `MV6_NR0=1`
  - [x] Add native Q8_0 draft-model support for Qwen3.5 0.8B: CPU dequant/matmul, Metal GEMV/top1, tied-output fallback when `output.weight` is absent, focused Q8_0 Metal spec, and reusable `--model` sync profiler
  - [x] Add first greedy speculative acceptance harness for Qwen35 target/draft pairs; serial verifier is correctness-only, but establishes acceptance-rate evidence before building a batched verifier
  - [x] Draft baseline: llama.cpp Qwen3.5 0.8B Q8_0 tg64 `145.53 tok/s` (`6.87 ms/tok`), native Q8_0 top1 `11.99 ms/tok`; greedy exact acceptance smoke ranges `13.33%` to `57.14%`, so exact greedy speculative needs either a faster draft, a closer draft, or a sampling-acceptance path before it can beat plain target decode
  - [x] Fix Q8_0 GEMV attribution block accounting and retune Q8_0 GEMV row parallelism; `MV_Q8_NR0=1` improves native 0.8B Q8_0 draft decode from `11.59 ms/tok` to `9.06 ms/tok` in the same 64-token profile, while `NR0=3/4` regress
  - [x] Add exact chunked target top1 verifier primitive (`prefill_tokens_top1s`) and harden shared Metal constant caching for mixed target+draft models; regression spec covers 9B target after 0.8B draft cache pollution
  - [x] Falsifier: chunking only the target verifier body is not enough for speedup with the current 0.8B draft. On `def fibonacci(n):`, gamma=4/tokens=24 has the same acceptance (`85.19%`) but serial verifier is `31.47 ms/tok` while chunk-body verifier is `32.97 ms/tok`; target verification time drops (`503.9` → `471.5 ms`) but fork/copy and per-row lm-head overhead erase it
  - [x] Falsifier: batched Q6_K lm-head top1 for verifier rows is exact but slower at gamma=4. On `def fibonacci(n):`, chunk verifier default is `30.03 ms/tok`; opt-in `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS=1` regresses to `32.44 ms/tok`, so keep row-batched top1 default-off
  - [x] Add exact `chunk-inplace` verifier mode that mutates target state directly and only restores a backup on rejection; on `def fibonacci(n):`, gamma=4/tokens=24 improves verifier wall from serial `29.09 ms/tok` and copy-back chunk `29.13 ms/tok` to `28.36 ms/tok`, but still trails plain target decode (`21.91 ms/tok`)
  - [x] Retune Q8_0 draft GEMV row parallelism from `MV_Q8_NSG=2` to `4` and make Q8 top1 dispatch width follow the Q8 simdgroup count; 0.8B Q8_0 draft decode improves from `~8.88-9.07 ms/tok` to `~8.36-8.38 ms/tok`, while `NSG=8/16/32` are slower or unstable
  - [x] After Q8_0 `NSG=4`, exact speculative `chunk-inplace` on `def fibonacci(n):` improves from `28.36 ms/tok` to `27.70 ms/tok` at the same `85.19%` acceptance, but still trails plain target decode (`21.96 ms/tok`)
  - [x] Add exact Q8_0 FFN gate/up dual GEMV for the 0.8B draft decode wave; it halves FFN-upgate dispatch count and improves interleaved draft decode from `8.54/8.85 ms/tok` with `QWEN35_Q8_DUAL_GEMV_OFF=1` to `8.44/8.51 ms/tok` default, while speculative `chunk-inplace` smoke improves draft time `231.0` -> `223.2 ms` over 32 generated tokens
  - [x] Remove repeated speculative harness rollback allocations by reusing preallocated target/draft backup states and report rollback timing; default prompt `gamma=4/tokens=32` improves from `23.83` to `22.66 ms/tok`, and `gamma=16/tokens=64` proves the high-acceptance path can beat plain target (`15.67` vs `22.24 ms/tok`)
  - [x] Add opt-in adaptive gamma (`--adaptive`, `--max-gamma`) as an exploration harness; with no-regrow-after-reject default, it helps high-acceptance prompts (`19.34 ms/tok` vs plain `22.33`) and avoids the earlier rejection-heavy collapse on `def fibonacci(n):` (`28.55 ms/tok` vs fixed gamma=4 `28.99`, plain target `21.61`)
  - [x] Add exact first-candidate early reject before draft span generation: if `draft_next != target_next`, skip draft backup, skip the rest of the draft candidates, advance one corrected target token, and continue from the corrected draft state. On `def fibonacci(n):`, gamma=4/tokens=64 improves from `32.43 ms/tok` with `QWEN35_SPEC_EARLY_REJECT_OFF=1` to `28.99 ms/tok` default in the same built harness
  - [x] Promote rejection-aware adaptive gamma to the speculative harness default, with `--no-adaptive` / `QWEN35_SPEC_ADAPTIVE=0` for fixed-gamma A/B. In the same built harness, default prompt improves from fixed `23.39 ms/tok` to adaptive `19.30 ms/tok`, and `def fibonacci(n):` improves from fixed `29.59 ms/tok` to adaptive `28.60 ms/tok`
  - [x] Add exact gamma=1 accepted-token fast path for chunk verifiers: when adaptive has fallen to one candidate and `draft_next == target_next`, advance target and draft directly without rollback backups or chunk verifier. On `def fibonacci(n):`, interleaved A/B improved from `27.93/32.02 ms/tok` with `QWEN35_SPEC_SINGLE_FAST_OFF=1` to `27.61/26.72 ms/tok` default
  - [x] Add exact target-only fallback once rejection-aware adaptive drops to low gamma. With `QWEN35_SPEC_PLAIN_FALLBACK_GAMMA=2` default, `def fibonacci(n):` exits speculative after the first rejection and measures `21.57 ms/tok` vs plain target `22.89` in the same final run, while high-acceptance prompt remains faster than plain target (`20.93` vs `23.51 ms/tok`)
  - [x] Raise adaptive `max_gamma` default from `16` to `32` while keeping initial `gamma=4`: final default-prompt smoke is `19.11 ms/tok` vs plain target `21.96`, while `def fibonacci(n):` still falls back after rejection and measures `21.21 ms/tok` vs plain target `21.94`; starting at `gamma=8` was rejected because it regressed `def fibonacci(n):` to `26.56 ms/tok`
  - [x] Re-check Q4_K dual FFN gate/up GEMV as a lower-level decode-kernel branch and reject it again: focused forward spec stayed green (`13 examples, 0 failures`), but paired A/B lost `0.367 ms/tok` (`0/6` wins) versus the separate-GEMV path, confirming this is register-pressure/occupancy bound rather than dispatch-bound
  - [x] Skip draft-state advance/resync before imminent target-only fallback (`QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` disables): early-reject `def fibonacci(n):` avoids one unused draft advance, and chunk-reject prompts avoid unused resync (`Once upon a time`: `22.28` vs `22.85 ms/tok`; `The quick brown fox`: `22.56` vs `22.84 ms/tok`); high-acceptance prompt has `draft_skip=0` and stays unchanged within noise
  - [x] Falsifier: deriving a faster local Q4_K_M draft from the downloaded 0.8B Q8_0 GGUF is blocked by llama.cpp (`requantizing from type q8_0 is disabled`); the partial output artifact was removed, so Q4 draft testing needs an F16/BF16 source or a separately downloaded Q4 GGUF
  - [x] Port eager Metal state-buffer preparation into `qwen35_generate` (`QWEN35_PREPARE_STATE_OFF=1` disables). The CLI now prepares target and neural-speculative draft/backup states before latency-sensitive prompt/decode sections, using the same `prepare_state_metal!` path measured in the benchmark harness.
  - [x] Falsifier/tuning note: initial `gamma=3` is not a safe default even though it is faster on the 100%-accept prompt (`~18.3 ms/tok` vs `~19.4`) and mixed partial-reject prompts (`Once upon a time` `21.56` vs gamma4 `22.42`), because it regresses `def fibonacci(n):` to `22.74 ms/tok`; `--max-gamma 16/24` did not recover the high-accept gain, so the effect is the initial chunk schedule, not just the cap
  - [x] Enable exact early-reject and gamma=1 accepted-token fast paths for `--verify serial` too: serial verifier is still not the default for high-accept prompts (`28.83 ms/tok` vs chunk `19.34`), but partial-reject profiling now avoids artificial draft/span waste (`Once upon a time` serial-fast `21.77` vs chunk `22.31`; `The quick brown fox` `21.77` vs chunk `22.41`)
  - [x] Add opt-in `--verify hybrid` mode: first speculative cycle uses serial verifier, later cycles use chunk-inplace. This helps first-cycle partial-reject prompts (`Once upon a time` `21.44` vs chunk `22.47`; `The quick brown fox` `21.73` vs `22.49`) but is not default because high-accept prompt slightly regresses (`19.32` vs `19.08`) and `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`21.68` vs `21.50`)
  - [x] Falsifier: a progressive verifier that serial-probes 1/2/3 first-cycle candidates and chunks the rest preserved exactness, but it is not worth carrying: high-accept regressed (`19.41-19.70 ms/tok` vs chunk `19.04`), partial-reject prompts were weaker than `hybrid`, and `def fibonacci(n):` regressed (`21.67-21.90` vs chunk `21.51`); temporary code was removed
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 mixed-size dual GEMV for draft recurrent `qkv + gate` lowers `rec.proj` encode accounting (`~3.00 ms` -> `~2.36-2.42 ms` over 16 tokens), but worsens end-to-end draft decode (`~7.85-7.88 ms/tok` separate GEMVs vs `~8.05-8.06 ms/tok` mixed), so this is another dispatch-saving/register-pressure tradeoff rather than a speed path
  - [x] Reuse the existing Q8_0 dual GEMV kernel for draft recurrent `alpha + beta` projections (`QWEN35_Q8_ALPHA_BETA_DUAL_GEMV_OFF=1` disables it): 0.8B draft sync profile improves from `8.77/8.90 ms/tok` off to `8.51/8.43 ms/tok` default over 64 tokens, while focused Qwen specs stay green
  - [x] Skip speculative draft-state backup when a future rejection is guaranteed to enter target-only fallback (`QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BACKUP_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` disables): exact harness output remains matched to greedy target, and partial-reject prompts avoid the unused draft copy (`draft_backup_skip=1`, `draft_backup=0.0 ms`)
  - [x] Add guarded fast regrowth after adaptive gamma reaches `8` (`QWEN35_SPEC_FAST_REGROW_MIN_GAMMA=0` disables): high-accept prompt now reaches `gamma=32` in `5` cycles instead of `7`, improving `The capital of France is` from `~19.2 ms/tok` to `~16.0-17.8 ms/tok`; `def fibonacci(n):` stays on the safe path (`max_seen=4`, fallback after reject)
  - [x] Reuse the existing Q8_0 dual GEMV kernel for full-attention `K + V` projections (`QWEN35_Q8_KV_DUAL_GEMV_OFF=1` disables): draft full.qkv encode drops from `~3.7 ms` to `~2.7 ms` over 64 tokens and high-accept speculative decode gets a small exact win (`15.73 ms/tok` off -> `15.68/15.71 ms/tok` default)
  - [x] Falsifier: switching fallback-bound low-gamma verifier cycles from `chunk-inplace` to forked `chunk` removes target backup but is not a win; high-accept regresses (`~15.8` -> `~16.0 ms/tok`) and reject prompts are neutral, so keep `chunk-inplace` default
  - [x] Falsifier: raising initial speculative `gamma` from `4` to `5` or `6` is worse under the current fast-regrow schedule; high-accept prompts slow slightly, `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`gamma=5` verifies one extra reject row, `gamma=6` breaks into many low-gamma cycles), and partial-reject prompts lose acceptance efficiency
  - [x] Falsifier: enabling batched lm-head top1 only for large verifier chunks (`>=8/16/24/32` rows) is still slower than per-row fused top1; high-accept target verifier rises from `~552-556 ms` to `~627-675 ms`, so leave `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS` default-off
  - [x] Refresh: lowering Q4_K pair H16 prefill threshold to b64 is still not a robust pp64 win; it halves FFN upgate conversion accounting but wall remains neutral/noisy (`~156.3-156.6 ms` default vs `~156.1-156.3 ms` branch under host load), so keep pair route gated at batch `>=256`
  - [x] CUDA raw-Q8 controller infrastructure: expose full-attention KV device buffers and add `QwenMixedStackRunner#copy_decode_state_to!` for recurrent conv/SSM plus K/V cache device-to-device copies.   - [x] CUDA same-stack raw-Q8 rollback: add `snapshot_decode_state` / `restore_decode_state` with optional KV coverage and route `--greedy-loop-probe-restore` through it.   - [x] CUDA raw-Q8 guarded chunk diagnostic: add `--greedy-loop-probe-chunk-gamma=N` plus margin fallback. This is controller accounting only; verifier remains sequential.
  - [x] Falsifier: duplicate-stack `--greedy-loop-probe-chunk-batched-verify` preserves exact output after switching verifier truth to materialized-logits scan, but it is not a speed path.   - [x] Falsifier: switching Q5_K batch GEMM from the current double-buffered schedule to a llama-style single-buffer schedule is exact but slower on pp64; focused specs pass (`22 examples, 0 failures`), but paired A/B loses (`159.99 ms` default vs `161.55 ms` single-buffer p50, default wins `7/8`), so keep Q5 double-buffering
  - [x] Falsifier: skipping full-attention KV copies in speculative target rollback is exact but not faster; focused rollback spec passed, yet high-accept smoke stayed neutral/slightly worse (`16.30 ms/tok` skip-KV vs `16.20 ms/tok` full backup) and `target_backup` remained `10.6 ms`, showing rollback cost is recurrent state buffers rather than KV
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 GEMV shared-X threadgroup staging is exact but slower for the 0.8B draft; the focused Q8 GEMV spec worsened (`~1.9 ms` -> `24.3 ms`) and draft decode regressed from `8.26` to `9.18 ms/tok`, so current Q8 speed is not limited by repeated x loads
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 block-major weight prepack is not a material GEMV speed path for the 0.8B draft on M2 Max.   - [x] Falsifier: making speculative gamma regrow more aggressive is not a safe default. `QWEN35_SPEC_FULL_ACCEPT_STREAK=1`, `QWEN35_SPEC_FAST_REGROW_MIN_GAMMA=4`, and initial `--gamma 8` all stayed neutral/slower on the 100%-accept prompt (`~16.4-16.9 ms/tok` vs default `16.44`) and regressed `def fibonacci(n):` (`24.75-24.97 ms/tok` vs default `22.98`), so keep the conservative current schedule
  - [x] Add opt-in staged speculative verifier mode for high-accept prompts (`--verify staged --stage-gate 4`). It drafts/verifies a guard stage before the rest of a large gamma cycle and preserves exact greedy output; with fixed `--gamma 32`, high-accept `The capital of France is` improves from default `16.45` to `15.04 ms/tok`, but rejection-sensitive `def fibonacci(n):` regresses to `32.58 ms/tok`, so the mode stays opt-in and not default
  - [x] Falsifier: Q6 lm-head top1 tile rows `12 -> 16` is correctness-safe but not a verifier win; focused specs passed (`13 examples, 0 failures`), yet high-accept target verifier stayed flat (`~573.8 ms` default, `~478-480 ms` staged) and reject default worsened (`22.84 -> 23.45 ms/tok`), so keep rows12
  - [x] Falsifier/fix: full-row batched Q6 lm-head verifier is fast but not exact because its F16 GEMM path can flip close argmax rows (`row24`: exact `220@8.900501` vs F16 full-row `198@8.8984375`).   - [x] Fix external speculative verifier fail-closed around guarded full-row target chunks: `qwen35_speculative_accept` now disables `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED` inside neural verifier/correction chunks unless `--allow-guarded-verifier` / `QWEN35_SPEC_ALLOW_GUARDED_VERIFIER=1` is explicit. Unsafe guarded mode matched llama.cpp on a high-repeat France prompt (`11.56 ms/tok`, `86.49 tok/s`, `100%` accept) but diverged under `bootstrap32`; the safe exact fix restores parity and a 3-prompt sweep completes without failures (`default` avg `1.153x`, `bootstrap32` avg `1.169x`, with bootstrap bad on code).
  - [x] Port the guarded-verifier fail-closed rule into `qwen35_generate` speculative chunks too; CLI neural speculative verification now disables `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED` around chunk verification/correction, matching the exact probe behavior.
  - [x] Add exact `--bootstrap-streak` / `QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_STREAK` policy control, `bootstrap32_s2` sweep preset, and the same env knob in `qwen35_generate`. Requiring two full-accept chunks before jumping to gamma32 avoids the code-prompt collapse from one-shot `bootstrap32`; the 3-prompt gate wins `3/3` vs default (`19.02 ms/tok` avg vs `19.64`, paired ratio `0.962x`), and a broader reps=2 8-prompt gate wins `9/16` with paired ratio `0.979x`.
  - [x] Add `ngram_bootstrap32_s2` sweep preset after exact n-gram front-running proved to be the strongest current policy. On an 8-prompt mix, `--ngram` alone improved paired ratio to `0.853x` vs default, and `ngram + bootstrap32_s2` stayed strong at `0.873x`; France reaches `~10.1-11.3 ms/tok`, faster than the local llama.cpp speculative run (`~11.75 ms/tok`) on the same target/draft pair.
  - [x] Add exact Q5_K/Q6_K prefill GEMM f32-output kernels that preserve the previous half-rounded output contract but skip the separate half-output → f32 conversion dispatch; also vectorize Q4_K H16 input tile loads. Focused specs pass (`23 examples, 0 failures`), standalone pp64 op attribution drops measured weighted hot-shape lower bound from `8.209 ms` to `2.913 ms`, while full pp64/pp256 wall remains host/noise limited around `153 ms` / `494 ms`
  - [x] Add matched benchmark mode for preallocated prompt state (`--native-prefill-prealloc`): it allocates GPU state once, resets only recurrent conv/SSM state between reps, and preserves exact `start_pos=0` output (`fresh=72:9.439041`, `reuse=72:9.439041`). Prompt64/gen64 smoke improves native prefill from `165.90 ms` p50 default to `156.27 ms` p50 preallocated, moving the matched gap from `-3.10%` to `-1.57%`; this is session-style state reuse/harness attribution, not a first-run kernel breakthrough.
  - [x] WBA/LTP next-frame saved: Qwen35 already uses wave scheduling, so the next breakthrough target is not "add WBA" but lift WBA from command-buffer groups to algebraic operator diamonds. Prime candidate is FFN `normed -> gate/up -> SwiGLU -> down -> residual` as a tile-streamed exact diamond, plus speculative verifier as a time-axis WBA batch.
  - [x] Falsifier: extending the existing in-place SwiGLU activation trick from prefill into decode wave is correctness-safe but not a default win. `QWEN35_DECODE_SWIGLU_INPLACE=1` passes targeted specs (`17 examples, 0 failures`) but paired decode A/B loses/noisy versus default (`25.135 ms/tok` default vs `25.786 ms/tok` opt-in; default wins `5/8`), so keep it default-off and treat full tile-streaming FFN as the real diamond, not buffer aliasing.
  - [x] Promote exact row-batched verifier top1 only for large chunks (`QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN`, default `8`; `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_OFF=1` disables): targeted specs pass (`17 examples, 0 failures`), high-accept speculative A/B wins `4/4` against rows-off (`16.29/21.04/21.13/19.90 ms/tok` auto vs `17.63/21.30/22.10/23.93 ms/tok` off), while rejection-heavy paths with `gamma=4` stay below the threshold and use the old per-row exact top1 path.
  - [x] Falsifier: making the old full-row Q6 lm-head route use f32 output/reduce still does not make it exact.   - [x] Falsifier: adaptive staged verifier and regrow scheduling are not default wins after row-batched top1. `--verify staged --stage-gate 8/16` regresses the high-accept prompt (`18.76 ms/tok` chunk-inplace vs `26.86/21.79`) and does not help rejection-heavy prompts; `QWEN35_SPEC_ADAPTIVE_REGROW=1` reintroduces expensive draft/resync work and regresses `def fibonacci(n):` (`23.77 -> 48.00 ms/tok`).
  - [x] Falsifier: naive prefix-copy of Metal KV buffers in `State.copy_from!` is not safe with the current state-position invariant.   - [x] Add opt-in speculative bootstrap jump (`--bootstrap-gamma N` / `QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_GAMMA`): on 100%-accept `The capital of France is`, bootstrap32 cuts cycles `5 -> 3` and improves `17.89 -> 15.10 ms/tok`; it remains default-off because `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`23.36 -> 29.93`) after an initially accepted prefix.
  - [x] Fresh matched llama.cpp benchmark: with preallocated native prompt state, pp64 prefill beats llama.cpp by `+35.53%` (`335.25 tok/s` p50 native vs `247.37 tok/s` llama.cpp); decode is effectively parity/slightly ahead at `+1.03%` (`16.32 tok/s` p50 native vs `16.15 tok/s` llama.cpp).
  - [x] Falsifier: approximate draft layer truncation is not a useful exact speculative speed path for the current 0.8B Q8_0 draft. The draft has `24` layers, so `--draft-layers 24/26/28` is the full model; the first real truncation tested (`22`) collapses high-accept prompts to immediate reject/fallback (`The capital of France is`: `100%` full-draft accept at `~15.55 ms/tok` vs `0%` accept and `~20.63 ms/tok` with 22 layers), while partial-reject prompts also lose acceptance.
  - [x] Falsifier: lowering exact row-batched verifier top1 threshold from `8` rows to `4` is not a safe default. A noisy but adversarial four-prompt smoke showed mixed results: `def fibonacci(n):` improved in that run (`32.61 -> 30.91 ms/tok`), but high-accept `The capital of France is` regressed (`19.58 -> 22.60 ms/tok`) and `The quick brown fox` regressed (`31.08 -> 33.63 ms/tok`), so keep `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN=8`.
  - [x] Add exact n-gram/cache speculative harness (`bin/qwen35_ngram_speculative.cr`) as a non-neural draft paradigm. It keeps target verification exact and proposes candidates only after a repeated suffix of at least 6 tokens; high-repeat `The capital of France is` accepts `55/55` n-gram candidates and measures `17.85 ms/tok` in the smoke, while `def fibonacci(n):` proposes no candidates and falls back to plain target steps instead of burning verifier chunks on weak 1-grams.
  - [x] Extract n-gram draft selection into reusable `ML::GGUF::NgramDraft` with focused specs, so the next step can compose n-gram draft with the neural Q8_0 draft without duplicating suffix-matching logic.
  - [x] Add recursive n-gram candidate extension: once a repeated suffix is found, the draft can extend through its own scratch history up to `gamma=32` while still being target-verified exactly. High-repeat `The capital of France is` improves from non-recursive `~17.46 ms/tok` / `7` verifier cycles to recursive `~9.0 ms/tok` / `2` cycles; no-repeat `def fibonacci(n):` still proposes no candidates and falls back to plain target steps.
  - [x] Add n-gram fail-closed guard: after the first rejected n-gram chunk, the harness disables further n-gram drafting by default (`--keep-ngram-after-reject` / `QWEN35_NGRAM_DISABLE_AFTER_REJECT_OFF=1` restores exploration). This preserves the high-repeat win and avoids repeated verifier waste on partial-repeat prompts.
  - [x] Compose n-gram/cache draft with the neural speculative harness behind `--ngram` / `QWEN35_SPEC_NGRAM=1`. The n-gram front-runner is exact target-verified, lazy-resyncs the Q8_0 draft only if neural drafting is needed afterward, and improves high-repeat `The capital of France is` from neural-only `17.08 ms/tok` to n-gram+neural `11.24 ms/tok` in the same built harness while leaving no-repeat adversary prompts effectively unchanged.
  - [x] Falsifier: target-only seeding before the first n-gram match is unsafe with the current chunk verifier. It improves high-repeat `The capital of France is` to `~8.85 ms/tok`, but adversary `The quick brown fox` diverges from greedy at token 31 even when row-batched top1 is disabled for the n-gram chunk, so no target-seed code was retained.
  - [x] Add opt-in exact n-gram speculative decode to the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_NGRAM_DECODE=1`). `qwen35_generate` now reuses `ML::GGUF::NgramDraft`, verifies chunks with the target path, fails closed after first n-gram rejection by default, and preserves exact greedy token IDs on both high-repeat `The capital of France is` (`55/55` n-gram accepted) and adversary `The quick brown fox` (`18/32` accepted then disabled).
  - [x] Add opt-in exact neural speculative decode to the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_SPECULATIVE_DECODE=1`, optional `QWEN35_DRAFT_MODEL`).   - [x] Falsifier/fix: composing n-gram and neural speculative inside `qwen35_generate` is not a speed win and exposed an exactness hazard when guarded full-row verification is applied to partial n-gram rejects.   - [x] Apply the same fail-closed n-gram verifier guard to the research speculative harness (`bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr`): n-gram chunks now temporarily disable `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED`, while neural chunks can still use the guard. Focused forward specs pass, high-repeat n-gram+guard smoke stays exact at `9.90 ms/tok`, and partial-repeat `The quick brown fox` stays exact with n-gram disabled after first reject.
  - [x] Fix thresholded row-batched verifier activation: `prefill_tokens_top1s` now always asks the routed output-head helper, which auto-enables exact row-batched top1 only for chunks at or above `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN=8` and still respects `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_OFF=1`. Current smoke on `The capital of France is` improves high-accept speculative wall from `17.0 ms/tok` with rows-off to `16.43-16.67 ms/tok` default; `def fibonacci(n):` and `The quick brown fox` stay exact and remain on the gamma-4/per-row fallback path.
  - [x] Add opt-in guarded full-row verifier accelerator (`QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED=1`): large verifier chunks run the fast full-row Q6/F16 path, compute top1/top2 margins, and exactly fall back through row-batched fused top1 for low-margin rows. It is not default because accepted fast rows still rely on the F16 margin guard, but exact harness smokes passed; high-accept `The capital of France is` improved to `14.32 ms/tok`, and n-gram+neural improved to `9.20 ms/tok` in fresh local runs.
  - [x] Add the first exact FFN WBA sub-diamond in decode wave: Q4_K/Q6_K FFN-down GEMV can now fold the final residual add into the GEMV output write (`QWEN35_FFN_DOWN_ADD_FUSED_OFF=1` disables). Focused specs pass, default-vs-off token IDs match on `The quick brown fox`, sync profile removes `rec.add/full.add` trace kernels and improves the short smoke (`24.82 ms/tok` default vs `29.57 ms/tok` off), while paired A/B under noisy load is small/neutral-to-positive (`+0.067..0.312 ms/tok`).
  - [x] Extend the exact FFN-down residual-add WBA path to Q8_0 draft GEMV: the 0.8B draft wave now emits `rec.ffn_down_add/full.ffn_down_add` instead of `ffn_down + add`. Focused Metal/forward specs pass, and paired 0.8B draft A/B improves from `8.257 ms/tok` off to `8.102 ms/tok` default (`7/8` wins), though full speculative wall is still target-verifier dominated.
  - [x] Add an env-tunable Q4 pair-H16 prefill threshold (`QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_MIN_BATCH`). Historical default `256` avoided noisy pp64/pp128 evidence, but after later H16 routing cleanups a refreshed threshold sweep supports default `64`: threshold64 beats threshold256 at pp64 (`150.05 ms` vs `150.38 ms`, `12/12` wins in the repeat) and pp128 (`263.66 ms` vs `264.07 ms`, branch wins `5/6`); default-vs-off remains small-positive (`pp64 -0.13 ms`, `5/8`; `pp128 -0.57 ms`, `5/6`).
  - [x] Falsifier: lowering `QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_MIN_BATCH` from `64` to `32` is not a useful short-prompt win. Prepared-state A/B was neutral at pp32 (`92.42` vs `92.40 ms`, wins `2/6`) and pp64 (`142.95` vs `142.87 ms`, wins `1/6`), and slightly worse at pp128 (`256.28` vs `256.50 ms`, wins `5/6` for default), so keep the current threshold at `64`.
  - [x] Falsifier/default-off feature: ported the same FFN-down residual-add WBA pattern to prefill chunk routes for Q6_K batch GEMM (`QWEN35_PREFILL_FFN_DOWN_ADD_FUSED=1` enables). Correctness specs pass in both default and opt-in modes, but paired prefill A/B does not justify default-on: pp64 regressed in the original lazy-state run, and a refreshed prepared-state check is neutral/negative at pp64 (`142.32 ms` default vs `142.43 ms` opt-in, `4/8`) and pp256 (`477.15 ms` default vs `478.33 ms` opt-in, `4/6`). Earlier Q4 batch-add attempts were rejected because cooperative stores lost the direct simdgroup-store advantage, and an accidental Q4 batch-to-GEMV route catastrophically slowed pp64/pp256; keep the safe Q6-only path opt-in only as a controlled experiment.
  - [x] Falsifier: caching the four per-lane `DeltaNet` post-norm `Y[d]` values in registers removes a second global read in theory, but it is not a wall win in the current post kernel. Focused Metal specs pass, yet pp64 worsened (`152.56 ms` old p50 vs `155.71 ms` patched p50) and pp256 was neutral (`492.55 ms` old vs `492.23 ms` patched), so the patch was removed; the next recurrent breakthrough must fuse the DeltaNet/post boundary at the scan level or change the scan algorithm, not just local-cache the post pass.
  - [x] Add explicit practical CLI decode policy (`QWEN35_DECODE_POLICY=greedy|ngram|speculative|auto`) and fail-fast legacy conflict handling. `auto` is intentionally conservative and maps to the exact fail-closed n-gram path for now; real CLI parity checks against greedy pass for `The capital of France is`, `The quick brown fox`, and `def fibonacci(n):` at 32 generated tokens, while setting both legacy mode envs without an explicit policy now errors before model load instead of silently choosing neural speculative.
  - [x] Falsifier: extending FFN-down residual-add fusion into the `full_attn_layer_chunk_project_last` prefill-final path is correctness-safe but not a speed win. Focused Qwen/Metal specs passed (`23 examples, 0 failures`), yet pp64 paired A/B slightly favored the old separate `ffn_down + add` route (`150.64 ms` fused vs `150.45 ms` old, fused wins `3/8`), so no source change was retained.
  - [x] Reduce exact n-gram draft overhead on no-candidate paths: recursive mode now probes the current history once and only duplicates/extends scratch history after a first match exists. Focused n-gram specs pass, an isolated 2048-token no-match microbench improves slightly (`370.43 -> 358.58 ms` over 5000 calls), and real `QWEN35_DECODE_POLICY=auto` parity still matches greedy on the three 32-token CLI smoke prompts.
  - [x] Port the harness-only speculative bootstrap jump into the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_GAMMA=N`, default off). It remains opt-in because earlier harness evidence showed high-accept speedups but regressions on prompts that reject after an accepted prefix; CLI parity smokes with bootstrap32 still match greedy token IDs on high-accept and rejection prompts.
  - [x] Port exact draft-backup/resync skip before target-only fallback into `qwen35_generate` (`QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` and `QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BACKUP_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` disable). Practical CLI parity matches greedy/default-off token IDs on high-accept and rejection prompts; the partial-reject smoke improves `The quick brown fox` from `25.99 -> 25.09 ms/tok` by avoiding one draft resync, while high-accept is neutral/noisy.
  - [x] Falsifier/narrowing: raising neural speculative `QWEN35_SPEC_PLAIN_FALLBACK_GAMMA` from default `2` to `8` is not a robust default win. It helps some `bootstrap_gamma=16` cases by skipping draft backups and falling back sooner after rejection (`The capital of France is` around `15.37 ms/tok`; `def fibonacci(n):` around `21.38 ms/tok` instead of `24.5` with fallback `2`), but no-bootstrap high-accept was neutral/noisy and one adjacent run regressed (`17.67` default-8 vs `17.50` old-2). Keep default `2`; use `fallback_gamma>=8` only with explicit bootstrap/high-accept tuning.
  - [x] Falsifier: disabling auto n-gram after 16 consecutive no-candidate plain steps preserves exact output but is not a useful speed policy. It leaves high-repeat behavior intact, but no-repeat `def fibonacci(n):` stayed slightly slower (`20.29 ms/tok` with miss-limit vs `20.22 ms/tok` without) and the policy can suppress late n-gram wins, so no source change was retained.
  - [x] Add the harness-compatible `QWEN35_SPEC_PLAIN_FALLBACK_OFF=1` escape hatch to `qwen35_generate` without changing defaults. The CLI now reports whether target-only fallback is enabled, and the skip-draft-before-fallback optimization is gated by the same fallback policy so fallback-off A/B remains semantically clean.
  - [x] Make the practical CLI n-gram rollback backup state lazy: `qwen35_generate` now allocates the backup only when a candidate chunk actually appears. This is exact and avoids a large unused state allocation on no-candidate auto/ngram runs; parity still matches greedy for high-repeat, partial-repeat, and no-repeat 32-token smokes, though wall timing is neutral/noisy.
  - [x] Add default-preserving CLI trace suppression (`QWEN35_TRACE_STEPS_OFF=1` or `QWEN35_QUIET=1`) for benchmark/generation runs. Summaries and final output stay visible, but per-token/per-cycle logs are suppressed; greedy, auto, and neural speculative 32-token parity smokes still match baseline token IDs on high-repeat, partial-repeat, and no-repeat prompts.
  - [x] Port the harness gamma=1 accepted-token fast path into `qwen35_generate` (`QWEN35_SPEC_SINGLE_FAST_OFF=1` disables). The practical CLI now bypasses rollback backup and chunk verification when `draft_next == target_next` at `gamma=1`, preserving exact token IDs; default target-only fallback means the path is mostly an A/B/control win for fallback-off speculative runs rather than a default speed lever.
  - [x] Align the neural speculative acceptance harness with practical CLI state setup: `bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr` now prepares target/draft/backup Metal state buffers by default and reports `prepare_state=...`; `QWEN35_PREPARE_STATE_OFF=1` preserves the old lazy-allocation path for A/B. High-accept 16-token smoke stays exact and reduces backup/copy timing noise (`target_backup ~2.2 ms`, `draft_backup ~0.4 ms` prepared vs `~5.3/~1.8 ms` lazy in the adjacent run).
  - [x] Fresh speculative attribution: high-accept neural speculation with bootstrap32/full-row guard reaches `11.49 ms/tok` for 64 generated tokens (`87.04 tok/s`, `100%` accepted), but time is now draft-dominated (`draft=451.3 ms`, `target_verify=274.6 ms`). Partial-reject `The quick brown fox` correctly falls back after one cycle and is parity/slightly slower than plain target (`22.33` vs `22.11 ms/tok`), so the next exact neural-spec win needs a faster/smaller draft or fewer draft steps, not just verifier micro-tuning.
  - [x] Falsifier: reducing Q8_0 top1 rows-per-threadgroup from `12` to `8` looked faster in draft sync profile (`~7.31-7.44 ms/tok` vs `~7.65-7.74 ms/tok` on repeated 64-token draft runs), but failed exact speculative parity on `The capital of France is` with bootstrap32/full-row guard. The source was reverted to `12`; Q8 top1 tile-shape changes require end-to-end speculative parity, not just draft-only speed.
  - [x] Falsifier: fusing Q8_0 dual FFN gate/up GEMV with SwiGLU activation removed the `ffn_act` trace rows but regressed draft wall. Focused specs passed with `QWEN35_Q8_FFN_SWIGLU_FUSED=1`, yet paired 64-token draft profiles favored the existing separate activation path (`~7.73-7.81 ms/tok` off vs `~7.75-8.18 ms/tok` fused). The temporary kernel/route was removed; like the earlier Q4 pair+SwiGLU branch, putting `exp` into the projection kernel is not a speed path.
  - [x] Add practical CLI neural verifier selection (`QWEN35_SPEC_VERIFY=chunk-inplace|hybrid|serial`). Default remains `chunk-inplace`; opt-in `hybrid` verifies the first speculative cycle serially and then returns to chunking, improving first-cycle partial-reject smokes (`Once upon a time` `22.84/23.06 -> 22.48/22.11 ms/tok`, `The quick brown fox` `22.86/22.85 -> 22.12/22.22`) while regressing high-accept prompts (`The capital of France is` `16.33/16.40 -> 16.94/16.72`).
  - [x] Falsifier: a Q8_0 mixed dual GEMV for unequal recurrent draft projections (`qkv 1024x6144` + `gate 1024x2048`) reduced `rec.proj` encode time but regressed 0.8B draft wall (`~7.39-7.47 ms/tok` separate vs `~7.62-7.70` mixed), so the temporary kernel/route was removed. Dispatch fusion alone is not enough for this Q8 projection shape.
  - [x] Add exact recurrent prefill shared-H16 projection input: Q5 qkv and Q4 gate GEMMs now share one F32→F16 conversion (`QWEN35_REC_PROJ_SHARED_H16_OFF=1` disables). Focused Qwen/Metal specs pass (`23 examples, 0 failures`); paired A/B is neutral at pp64 (`150.45 ms` default vs `150.55 ms` off, `4/8` wins) and small-positive at pp256/pp1024 (`483.28 vs 484.48 ms`, `4/6`; `1916.54 vs 1917.83 ms`, `3/3`) while reducing profiled conversion traffic by one recurrent `4096*b` conversion per recurrent layer.
  - [x] Re-check llama.cpp Q4_K prefill GEMM source against the current local Q4_H16 route. llama.cpp still uses the same 64x32/NK32/4-simdgroup simdgroup-matrix `kernel_mul_mm_q4_K_f32` family; the local route already keeps the same tile shape and adds prepared F16 input reuse. Prepared-state pp64 A/B confirms the H16 route is still material (`145.99 ms` default vs `148.07 ms` with `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_GEMM_OFF=1`, `6/6` wins), so there is no obvious llama.cpp Q4 tile trick left to copy.
  - [x] Add opt-in intra-group prefill phase profiling (`QWEN35_PREFILL_PHASE_PROFILE=1`) for `full+rec` grouped command buffers. It intentionally inserts profiling command-buffer boundaries after the full-attention layer and after each recurrent layer, so it is attribution-only, not a production route. pp64 prepared profile shows each `full+rec` group is almost exactly four similar phases: full layer `~4.39-4.46 ms`, each recurrent layer `~4.50-4.61 ms`; default env-off profile remains unchanged around `142.96 ms` p50. This refutes a hidden single bad phase inside the grouped wave.
  - [x] Falsifier: exact compact DeltaNet summaries do not currently beat the production rowwise chunk even as a final-state lower bound. `qwen35_deltanet_compact_vs_rowwise_micro` measured compact vs rowwise p50 at block8 `0.3375` vs `0.2665 ms`, block16 `0.4396` vs `0.2831 ms`, and block32 `0.3536` vs `0.2636 ms`; row-threadgroup apply was also slower/neutral. Row-basis summary composition itself is exact and tileable, but the block-scan model says rank growth/composition cost only becomes attractive for long prompts with a much better integrated compact scan. Do not integrate compact summaries into first-run prefill yet.
  - [x] Falsifier: Q4_K per-subblock 16-entry dequant lookup tables are exact but slower. A temporary `dequantize_q4_K_fn` LUT variant passed focused Qwen/Metal specs (`23 examples, 0 failures`), but hot-shape attribution for Q4 `4096x12288 b64` worsened from `1.600 ms` after reverting to the current arithmetic path to `3.748 ms` with the LUT; the smaller Q4 `4096x4096 b64` shape also worsened (`0.661 ms` current vs `0.753 ms` LUT). Do not add nibble lookup/precompute inside the current Q4 GEMM kernel; register/local pressure dominates the saved multiply.
  - [x] Falsifier: changing Q4_K GEMM dequant arithmetic from float to half is neutral and weakens numeric margin. A temporary half-arithmetic `dequantize_q4_K_fn` passed focused specs, but `metal_q4k_gemm` max diff increased to `0.0012678653`; hot-shape attribution was effectively flat on Q4 `4096x12288 b64` (`3.032 ms` half vs `3.067 ms` reverted current) and worse on Q4 `4096x4096 b64` (`0.164 ms` half vs `0.147 ms` current). Keep the current float expression with one final half rounding.
  - [x] Falsifier: removing inner `simdgroup_barrier(mem_none)` calls from Q5/Q6 f32-output prefill GEMM kernels is not a win. A temporary barrier-free Q5/Q6 patch passed focused specs (`23 examples, 0 failures`), but spec timings showed large regressions (`metal_q5k_gemm` `176.8 ms`, `metal_q6k_gemm` `45.1 ms`), and hot-shape attribution worsened Q6 `12288x4096 b64` to `0.897 ms` and Q5 `4096x8192 b64` to `0.503 ms` versus current baselines around `0.757/0.414 ms`. Keep the barriers, matching the earlier Q4 barrier falsifier.
  - [x] Falsifier: resynchronizing the neural draft immediately after a CLI early-reject is not a speed win when target-only fallback is disabled. A temporary `qwen35_generate` patch made fallback-off `def fibonacci(n):` acceptance jump from `4/32` to `31/32`, but wall regressed from `23.63` to `27.36 ms/tok`; `Once upon a time` similarly regressed from `28.02` to `33.04 ms/tok`. Keep the current fail-cheap behavior: after low-confidence early rejects, spending draft cycles to improve acceptance can be slower than letting target steps dominate.
  - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_speculative_sweep.cr`, a measurement-only multi-prompt policy sweep for the speculative acceptance harness. It runs a compiled `qwen35_speculative_accept` runner across named policies (`default`, `guard`, `bootstrap32`, `bootstrap32_guard`, `hybrid`, `ngram`, `ngram_guard`), parses `ms/tok`, acceptance, gamma stats, and plain-target baseline, then prints per-prompt rows plus policy averages. The sweep now interleaves policies inside each prompt/repetition block to reduce host-drift bias; an interleaved smoke with `--tokens 8 --reps 2 --policies default,bootstrap32,ngram` ranked default and n-gram near parity (`1.274x`/`1.267x`) while bootstrap32 lagged (`1.213x`), demonstrating why policy changes need multi-prompt wall-time sweeps instead of one-prompt acceptance claims.
  - [x] Add `--dump-cycles JSONL` to the neural/ngram speculative harness. `bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr` now writes per-cycle JSONL with target/draft model ids, prompt hash, position, policy/kind, verifier mode, gamma, proposed/accepted counts, reject index, n-gram match features, timing breakdowns, candidate hash, and post-run `expected_gain_ms` versus plain target ms/token. Raw token ids are omitted by default and require explicit `--dump-cycle-token-ids` / `QWEN35_SPEC_DUMP_TOKEN_IDS=1`. Verified with high-accept neural, token-id opt-in, and forced n-gram-cycle smokes.
  - [x] Fix speculative harness baseline timing: `plain_target_wall` is now target decode-only after its own prompt prefill, matching `spec_wall`, and `plain_target_prefill_wall` is printed separately. Previous `qwen35_speculative_accept` speedup and `expected_gain_ms` numbers that used prefill-included plain baselines are stale; parity/acceptance counts remain useful.
  - [x] Wire cycle dumps into the multi-prompt policy sweep: `bin/qwen35_speculative_sweep.cr --dump-cycles-dir DIR` now forwards per-run `--dump-cycles` paths named by rep/prompt/policy, optionally forwards `--dump-cycle-token-ids`, and reports dump file counts. A 2-prompt/2-policy smoke produced 4/4 JSONL files, including both neural and n-gram cycle records.
  - [x] Add cycle-level router diagnostics to the sweep output. When `--dump-cycles-dir` is active, the sweep parses produced JSONL files and prints aggregate `policy/kind` cycle counts, accepted/proposed totals, reject counts, summed expected gain, and timing totals. A smoke summarized `neural/neural` and `ngram/ngram` rows separately, giving immediate signal for router feature work without a separate notebook.
  - [x] Add prompt labels and a privacy-preserving manifest to cycle-dump sweeps. `--prompt` / `--only-prompts` entries can now use `NAME::TEXT`; dump filenames include the safe prompt label, and `prompt_manifest.jsonl` records `prompt_index`, `prompt_name`, prompt hash, and byte length. Raw prompt text remains opt-in via `--dump-prompt-texts`; default sweep output now prints labels instead of raw prompt previews.
  - [x] Add named warm-aware gamma policies to the sweep: `router16` (`gamma4`, cap `max_gamma=16`, jump to `16` after one full accept), `fixed16`, and n-gram variants. These are measurement aliases over existing exact verifier controls, not new decode semantics. Fake-runner and real-runner smokes verified policy selection, labeled dump filenames, and cycle summaries.
  - [x] Add named staged large-gamma policies to the sweep: `staged16` and `ngram_staged16` use fixed `gamma16` with `--verify staged --stage-gate 4`, so the verifier can stop after a bad first stage instead of always paying for a full wrong tail. On the 27B+2B `gen16` four-prompt gate, staged improved low-accept prompts relative to fixed16 (`France` `0.434x -> 0.680x`, `quick_fox` `0.563x -> 0.607x`) but regressed high-accept prompts relative to full chunk; `ngram_staged16` was near default by paired delta (`+0.66ms/tok`) and still needs a router.
  - [ ] Build a local acceptance/gain dataset for 27B target with 0.8B and 2B drafts. Start with `200-500` prompts across code, chat/facts, repetition/templates, JSON/markdown/logs, and known adversaries (`The capital of France is`, `Once upon a time`, `The quick brown fox`, `def fibonacci(n):`). Labels are generated automatically by exact target verification: `accepted_count`, `accept>=3/4`, and `expected_gain_ms > 0`.
    - [x] Dataset preflight: local 27B target (`Qwen3.6-27B-Q4_K_M`) is vocab-compatible with both local drafts (`Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0` and `Qwen3.5-2B-Q4_K_M`). Short `--tokens 2 --gamma 2 --dump-cycles` smokes on `The capital of France is` preserved greedy parity and wrote JSONL labels for both drafts. This only verifies compatibility/data path, not broad acceptance quality.
    - [x] Corrected 27B router-data mini-gate: 4 prompts (`France`, forced repetition, `def fibonacci`, `quick brown fox`), `tokens=4`, policies `default,ngram`, exact parity preserved through the runner. With decode-only plain baseline, both external drafts are slower than target-only decode on this narrow slice; 2B still beats 0.8B on speculative wall and acceptance (`default`: `92.62ms/tok`, `87.5%` vs `102.93ms/tok`, `81.25%`; `ngram`: `83.76ms/tok`, `87.5%` vs `97.56ms/tok`, `81.25%`). Treat this as router-data and a verifier-cost falsifier, not a speed win.
    - [x] Verifier route/gamma falsifier: on a 27B + 2B high-accept `def fibonacci` prompt, `chunk-inplace` is the best current verifier mode but still slower than target-only decode. At `tokens=8`, fixed `gamma=4/8/16` with `100%` acceptance measured `96.30/73.56/68.23ms/tok` versus plain decode around `56ms/tok`; target verifier alone was `677.8/507.4/466.5ms` before draft cost. External full-draft speculation cannot be the next speed path until verifier chunks beat serial decode or draft work is much cheaper/hidden.
    - [x] Add default-on target verifier warmup in the research acceptance harness. It warms `prefill_tokens_top1s` on a forked target state before decode timing and reports `verifier_warmup_wall` separately; `--no-warm-verifier` / `QWEN35_SPEC_WARM_VERIFIER_OFF=1` preserve cold A/B. This converts 27B high-accept `def fibonacci` `gamma16/tokens8` from slower-than-plain to faster (`0.8B`: `47.17ms/tok` vs `56.31`; `2B`: `48.24ms/tok` vs `56.18`), but the warmup itself costs about `417-459ms`, so it is a throughput/long-run lever, not a first-token-latency win.
    - [x] Warm-aware gamma gate: fixed `gamma16/tokens8` with 2B draft wins high-accept prompts but loses the 4-prompt mix (`default 0.849x`, `ngram 0.909x`) because `France` and partial `quick_fox` rejects dominate. Adaptive `gamma4->16` is safer but still sub-break-even mixed (`default 0.929x`, `ngram 0.985x`), and bootstrap16-after-one-full-accept is effectively flat (`default 0.922x`, `ngram 0.995x`) at this short length. Router policy must avoid low-accept chunks and reserve large gamma for longer/high-confidence spans.
    - [x] Longer `gen16` warm-router gate: on 27B+2B over `France`, repeated alpha, `def fibonacci`, and `quick_fox`, `ngram_router16` was the best measured policy by paired delta (`-4.40ms/tok` vs default, wins `3/4`) and strong on repeated/code prompts (`2.727x`, `1.486x`). It is still not a default: `quick_fox` regressed (`0.647x`), and fixed `gamma16` was unsafe despite high-accept wins because low-accept prompts dominate tail cost. Next router needs an explicit first-chunk risk gate before enabling large-gamma continuation.
    - [x] Add reusable staged-router sweep aliases after the extra-args experiment: `router16_staged` and `ngram_router16_staged` combine the warm `gamma4->16` router with `--verify staged --stage-gate 4`. Fake-runner arg capture verified the forwarded flags, a 9B smoke preserved runner compatibility, and a 27B+2B no-dump `gen16` 4-prompt gate put `ngram_router16_staged` at paired delta `-5.04ms/tok` versus default, wins `3/4`, average ratio `0.889x`. It remains research-only: repeated alpha is the main gain (`2.65x`), code is only slightly faster (`1.138x`), and low-accept `quick_fox` still regresses (`0.776x`).
    - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_spec_router_dataset.cr`, a local JSONL extractor for learned-router training rows. It reads sweep `--dump-cycles-dir` outputs plus `prompt_manifest.jsonl`, reconstructs `sweep_policy`, adds derived labels (`accepted_ratio`, `full_accept`, `accept_ge_75pct`, `positive_gain`), preserves privacy by using prompt labels/hashes rather than raw prompt text, and prints policy/kind summaries. Smokes on the repeat-alpha and 4-prompt dump dirs produced `8` and `77` rows respectively.
    - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_spec_router_train.cr`, an offline logistic-regression baseline for router rows. On the tiny current combined dataset (`85` rows from repeat-alpha plus the 4-prompt staged-router dump), `positive_gain` training reached holdout accuracy `0.647` / precision `0.600` / recall `0.750`, while `full_accept` reached `1.000` holdout on this small deterministic split. Treat this as a pipeline smoke and feature-sanity check, not a production router quality claim; the next gate needs a larger prompt suite and less policy-leaky features.
    - [x] Harden the offline router trainer against obvious leakage: add `--include-kind`, `--exclude-kind`, and `--no-sweep-feature` so training can drop fallback rows and remove `sweep_policy` as a categorical feature. On the same tiny dataset with `--exclude-kind target_only,neural_early_reject --no-sweep-feature`, `positive_gain` holdout dropped to `0.571` accuracy (`0.750` precision / `0.600` recall) and `full_accept` to `0.714` accuracy. This is the expected adversary signal: the previous high score was partly policy/row-mix leakage, and the next useful step is broader data, not runtime integration.
    - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_spec_router_prompt_suite.cr`, a synthetic non-private prompt-suite generator for router data collection. It emits `218` prompts across fact, code, repeat/template, structured, chat, math, and optimization-template categories, with `--format sweep` output directly accepted by `qwen35_speculative_sweep --only-prompts`. A fake-runner sweep smoke over the first five prompts verified label parsing and pipe-delimiter safety without model load.

The staged-router alias won `11/12` paired rows by wall (`avg delta -4.71ms/tok`, avg ratio `0.963x`) and improved average spec wall `79.21 -> 74.50ms/tok`, but both policies remained below plain target on average (`0.809x` and `0.830x`) because low-accept fact/code tails dominate. Extractor produced `43` cycle rows; adding them to the tiny training set worsened filtered `positive_gain` holdout to `0.545` accuracy, confirming we need much more balanced data before runtime use.
    - [x] Run the repeat-category contrast shard: 27B+2B, all `8` repeat prompts, `tokens=16`, policies `default,ngram_router16_staged`, dumped to `/tmp/qwen35_router_suite_27b_2b_repeat_t16`. Here `ngram_router16_staged` is a real speed path: average spec wall `53.43 -> 37.35ms/tok`, average speedup versus plain target `1.081x -> 1.959x`, paired wins `6/8`, avg ratio `0.683x`. Two structured-repeat cases (`yaml_hosts`, `json_pairs`) still regressed, so the useful router boundary is "strong repeated suffix with accepted n-gram", not repeat category alone. Extractor produced `75` rows; filtered `positive_gain` holdout over the combined small set stayed weak (`0.588` accuracy), so runtime integration remains blocked on broader data/features.
    - [x] Add `prompt_category` to router rows and train with it as a cheap pre-decode feature. Re-extracting the current dump dirs produced `203` rows; filtered training with category enabled (`--exclude-kind target_only,neural_early_reject --no-sweep-feature`) improved `positive_gain` holdout from `0.706` accuracy / `0.706` precision / `1.000` recall without category to `0.765` accuracy / `0.786` precision / `0.917` recall with category. This is still small-data evidence, but it confirms prompt class is useful and not just protocol decoration.
    - [x] Add `--holdout-by prompt` to the offline trainer so cycles from the same prompt cannot leak across train/holdout. On the current small combined set, prompt-group holdout is highly unstable (`holdout-every=3`: `0.792` accuracy; `4`: `0.571`; `5`: `0.000`), which is exactly the warning we needed: row-level metrics are not enough, and the next quality gate must report prompt-group splits over a larger suite.
    - [x] Run the structured-category contrast shard: 27B+2B, all `8` structured prompts, `tokens=8`, policies `default,ngram_router16_staged`, dumped to `/tmp/qwen35_router_suite_27b_2b_structured_t8`. This falsifies "structured output means n-gram win": staged n-gram lost on average (`70.62 -> 74.89ms/tok`, speedup `0.818x -> 0.780x`) despite winning `6/8` tiny paired rows, because `structured_dockerfile` and the rejected n-gram chunk dominate. Extractor produced `112` rows; adding this shard made prompt-group holdout every-3 look sane (`0.808` accuracy), but it is still a small split and not a deployment gate.
    - [x] Run the math-category contrast shard: 27B+2B, all `6` math prompts, `tokens=8`, policies `default,ngram_router16_staged`, dumped to `/tmp/qwen35_router_suite_27b_2b_math_t8`. Math behaves like structured/fact, not repeat: staged n-gram lost on average (`63.22 -> 66.97ms/tok`, speedup `0.930x -> 0.875x`) and the n-gram rows were negative (`1/4` accepted, `-215.3ms` expected gain). On the current combined rows, `min_gain=0` had decent precision but selected `-2165ms` total gain; `min_gain=25` selected `+203.6ms` total gain with holdout accuracy `0.800`, precision `0.667`, recall `1.000`. Caveat: selected n-gram rows are still negative overall (`-1007.7ms`), so this is an offline gate, not runtime-ready.
    - [x] Threshold sweep for the `min_gain=25` model found a safer offline operating point: threshold `0.8` selected `23/109` filtered rows, total selected gain `+1042.0ms`, precision `0.870`, recall `0.571`; prompt-holdout metrics at the same threshold were `0.833` accuracy / `0.889` precision / `0.667` recall. The model effectively chooses only repeat-category rows; n-gram selected gain is barely positive (`+11.4ms`) and remains the weakest part of the policy.
    - [x] Run the chat-category shard: 27B+2B, all `8` chat prompts, `tokens=8`, policies `default,ngram_router16_staged`, dumped to `/tmp/qwen35_router_suite_27b_2b_chat_t8`. Chat behaves like a negative/neutral class for this policy: staged-router regressed average wall (`69.03 -> 71.03ms/tok`) and speedup (`0.839x -> 0.802x`), with no n-gram cycles. Adding chat rows left the conservative `min_gain=25/th=0.8` model selecting zero chat rows and the same `+1042.0ms` offline selected gain, which is the desired fail-closed behavior on this shard.
    - [x] Run the first template/ML-optimization shard: 27B+2B, first `12` template prompts, `tokens=8`, policies `default,ngram_router16_staged`, dumped to `/tmp/qwen35_router_suite_27b_2b_template12_t8`. This category is mostly negative/neutral: staged-router slightly regressed average wall (`77.09 -> 78.23ms/tok`) and produced only one positive n-gram cycle. This is still an associative affine transform over state, but naive dense block summaries would be too expensive; the viable research path is compact low-rank/WY-style block summaries plus per-block serial replay, not direct `conv1d/cumsum`.

## Deferred research backlog — efficient attention / long context

- [ ] **R.1** FlashAttention-style exact full-attention prefill kernel for Qwen35 full-attn layers; first add intra-group phase attribution and only implement if `full.attn` is material at pp2048+.
  - [x] Falsifier/current priority: pp2048 prefill attribution shows full-attention is not the wall yet (`attn wait 26.35 ms` inside `~10.17 s` profiled wall). The material wall is recurrent grouped prefill / DeltaNet serial work (`dn wait ~9991.69 ms`) plus large conversion traffic (`10.85 GiB`, 73% logical traffic), so FlashAttention is deferred until recurrent scan and conversion pressure are addressed.
- [ ] **R.2** DeepSeek Sparse Attention / NSA-style indexer experiment; requires calibration + sparse adaptation, not an exact drop-in for existing Qwen weights.
- [ ] **R.3** Linear-attention replacement track; treat as new architecture or distillation/continued-training project, not an inference-only optimization.
- [ ] **R.4** Training-free long-context inference hacks under eval gates: attention sinks / sliding window, SnapKV/H2O-like KV retention, and KV quantization. Keep all default-off until LongBench/RULER/top-logit drift evidence exists.
- [ ] **R.5** Exact DeltaNet associative block-scan prototype for long prefill.
  - [x] First prove equivalence on tiny CPU shapes (`s=8`) against the serial `delta_net_step!` recurrence: `spec/qwen35_deltanet_affine_scan_spec.cr` verifies both composed affine final state and block-prefix replay intermediate outputs (`2 examples, 0 failures`).
  - [x] Estimate work for `s=128` and prompt lengths `64/256/1024/2048` with a `1.25x` continuation gate: `bin/qwen35_deltanet_scan_model.cr` shows naive dense summaries fail, while compact summaries can pass pp256 if one summary composition stays below about `38-47` serial-token-equivalent DeltaNet steps for block sizes `8/16/32`.
  - [x] Add a dense CPU block-scan baseline (`bin/qwen35_deltanet_dense_scan_cpu.cr`) for exactness/overhead checks before Metal work. It reproduces serial final state on `s=16,tokens=64,block=16` and `s=32,tokens=128,block=32` with max state deltas below `1e-16`, but sequential CPU dense summary+replay is `~12-19x` slower than serial, confirming dense summaries are only plausible as a parallel long-prefill GPU baseline, not a short-prompt CPU-style algorithm.
  - [x] Extract reusable block-scan algebra into `ML::GGUF::Qwen35DeltaNetBlockScan`, so the proof spec and dense baseline share the same serial, affine, composition, replay, and compact-additive-summary formulas before any Metal prototype.
  - [x] Prototype compact block summaries using dense `A` plus low-rank `B = Σ left_i ⊗ right_i`, including compact apply and compact summary composition without materializing dense `B`. Focused specs pass (`8 examples, 0 failures`); sequential CPU compact is still slower than serial but faster than dense-summary CPU on checked shapes, and the storage model becomes favorable for Qwen shape `s=128` with block sizes below `64`.
  - [x] Prototype compact/WY-style transition summaries for `A`: `A = gamma * (I + U V^T)` now has exact build, apply, and composition checks. Focused specs pass (`11 examples, 0 failures`); the `--fully` CPU baseline avoids dense `A/B` materialization and is exact, but still sequentially slower than serial. This keeps the Metal path alive while confirming CPU-style execution is not the win.

The two-kernel `coeffs + apply` probe is exact within f32 drift (`max_delta < 3e-8`) and measures p50 `~0.257/0.269/0.293/0.322 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`; this excludes summary construction and prefill integration, so it is a green apply-kernel gate, not a full scan speedup claim.

It is exact within f32 drift but only becomes faster than CPU construction at larger blocks: p50 `~0.223/0.281/0.442/1.108 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`, with CPU-build speedup `0.07x/0.24x/2.13x/1.24x`. This says block `32` is the first plausible Metal construction point; block `8/16` need fusion with apply/replay or they lose to dispatch/setup overhead.

The direct per-rank vector kernel is exact within f32 drift (`max_right_delta < 5e-8`) and measures p50 `~1.206/1.086/2.034/2.835 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`; this makes compact-`B` construction the dominant current overhead and points to block `16` as the best early candidate before replay/scan costs.

The synthetic `build(A+B)+apply(final state)` path is exact within f32 drift, but at `s=128` block `8/16/32` p50 is `~1.300/2.324/3.566 ms` versus rowwise `~0.270/0.287/0.318 ms`. Since rowwise also produces per-token outputs and compact currently does not, do not integrate this compact-summary design into prefill without a new fused construction/replay algorithm.
  - [x] Apply the LTP/WBA frame to compact-`B` construction: a threadgroup-wide right-factor kernel parallelizes the `s=128` vector recurrence and remains exact within f32 drift (`max_right_tg_delta ~3.2e-8`). At block `16`, the microbench improved scalar compact-`B` p50 from `~2.18 ms` to `~0.30 ms` in a formatted rerun (`~7.2x`), with earlier short sweeps showing `~4.8-11.3x` over blocks `8/16/32`. This validates compact-`B` as an LTP/WBA target.

Blocks `1/4` still lose or remain marginal, so this is long-prefill-only evidence.

An optimistic exact rank cap at `s=128` makes `pp1024/pp2048` plausible (`~1.71x/~2.66x`) but still does not pass `1.25x` at `pp256`; the next prototype must include exact rank/basis compression or avoid rank-growing summary composition entirely.

  - [x] Follow-up low-rank DeltaNet sweep: rerunning with enough calibration vectors for true rank-64 (`tokens=128`, `calib=64`) improves held-out K residuals versus rank-32 (`p50 0.328` vs `0.475`, `p90 0.511` vs `0.698`) and preserves short greedy parity on a mixed code/markdown prompt. Approximating sparse recurrent layers `0,8,16` gives `98.44%` teacher-forced top1 and `100%` 12-token greedy top1 for both rank-32/64; approximating early recurrent layers `0,1,2,4,5,6` gives `100%` teacher-forced and `100%` 12-token greedy top1, with rank-64 reducing KL/max-delta (`mean_kl 0.0036` vs `0.0121`).
