If `K≈Bc`, the DeltaNet state can be stored as `M B^T` with shape `128 x rank` and outputs use `M*(B^T q)`, giving a plausible approximate speed path independent of exact associative scan.
  - [x] Prove the projected-K low-rank DeltaNet state formula on CPU: for fixed per-head basis `B` and `K_proj = Bc`, the full projected recurrence is exactly represented by `S = M B^T`, update `M = gM + beta*(V - gM c)c^T`, and output `Y = M*(B^T Q)`. The probe verifies low-rank `M` against full projected `128x128` recurrence with proof drift around `1e-8` for outputs and `<=1.15e-5` for reconstructed state. Adversary mixed code prompt is weaker: layers `0/2/4` keep `95.83%` top1 and mean cosine `0.9974..0.9991`, while layer1 rank24 drops to `91.67%` top1 / `0.9940` mean cosine; rank32 with longer calibration only improves layer1 to `93.75%`. Leaf rescue alone is insufficient; the next tree experiment must avoid paying for the wrong tail.

With the same conservative unit costs, progressive `4,4,8` estimates jump from `~1.17x/1.09x` (sum model) to `1.53x` on `varied` and `1.41x` on `math_paris`. TAINT/REFUTED 2026-04-27: later review/reruns did not reproduce the 3x timing (`~1.07-1.20x` instead), and this is an independent known-span layer-chunk proxy, not final autoregressive draft throughput or the requested `self-draft(N+1) + validator(N)` scheduler.
  - [x] Add the first draft/verifier overlap proxy (`--simulate-lowrank-metal-verifier-overlap`): submit one async low-rank full-layer chunk, run exact `prefill_tokens_top1s` on the held-out candidate span, then wait the draft lane. Correctness held (`draft_output_max=0`, verifier top1s matched), but measured overlap was essentially zero (`9B`: draft `2.94ms`, verifier `143.5ms`, serial `146.5ms`, overlap `146.5ms`; `27B`: draft `4.69ms`, verifier `352.0ms`, serial `356.7ms`, overlap `356.6ms`). This falsifies the idea that async draft alone is enough: the current exact verifier path is synchronous/internal-wait heavy and/or serialized on the same Metal queue. Next gate is an async exact verifier chunk/top1 path, not more draft-layer fusion.
  - [x] Add lane-local Metal command queues and a queued exact decode-wave verifier proxy (`--simulate-lowrank-metal-decode-verifier-overlap`). Known candidate tokens can be submitted through `forward_top1_async` without waiting between tokens because state dependencies live in ordered Metal buffers. Correctness held (`lr_decode_verify_match=true`, `draft_output_max=0`). Queued exact decode improved over serial decode (`9B/8 tokens`: `167.3ms -> 143.2ms`, `1.17x`; `27B/8 tokens`: `467.1ms -> 439.9ms`, `1.06x`), but overlap with a single low-rank layer chunk remained negligible (`hidden_ms~0.4-0.5ms`, `overlap_speedup~1.00x`). This means the verifier async primitive works, but a one-layer draft segment is too small relative to the verifier; next proxy should queue a layer-set/full-draft replay or tree batch, not just layer0.
  - [x] Add WBA timeline tracing for the decode-verifier overlap proxy (`QWEN35_WBA=1`). The timeline shows the real bottleneck is verifier submission/encoding, not draft math: on 9B/8 tokens, verifier submit grows from `~2-4ms` to `~17-19ms` per later token and waits consume `~60ms`; draft submit is `~0.5ms` and draft wait is almost fully hidden (`0.024ms`). On 27B/8 tokens, verifier submit is `~52-68ms` for later tokens, verifier wait is `~94ms`, and draft wait can become visible (`16.5ms`) due resource contention. LTP should therefore target verifier batching/layer-group submission and fewer per-token decode-wave encodes before trying more queues or more draft fusion.
  - [x] Add exact verifier LTP proxy (`--simulate-exact-verifier-ltp`) comparing serial decode waves, queued decode waves, and chunk-major `prefill_tokens_top1s` on the same held-out candidate span. Top1 ids match for queued and chunk-major routes. For 8-token spans, chunk-major verifier beats serial decode by `1.52x` on 9B (`168.6ms -> 110.7ms`) and `1.45x` on 27B (`469.8ms -> 323.4ms`), while queued decode gives only `1.07-1.15x`. This confirms the next production step: async/no-wait chunk-major verifier, not more per-token decode-wave scheduling.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 kernel gate before resuming self-draft/validator parallelization: inventory found one unused added Metal entrypoint (`lowrank_delta_chunk_step`) with no Crystal wrapper or probe coverage, so it was removed. Current added acceleration kernels are `lowrank_project_coeffs`, `lowrank_project_coeffs_chunk`, `lowrank_delta_step`, and `lowrank_delta_chunk_step_parallel`. Rebuilt `/tmp/qwen35_probe_kernel_gate` and reran 9B layer2 ranks `32,48,64` with `--simulate-lowrank-metal`, `--simulate-lowrank-metal-chunk`, `--simulate-lowrank-metal-chunk-out`, `--simulate-lowrank-metal-layer-chunk`, and `--simulate-lowrank-metal-layer-full`: all CPU-vs-Metal checks stayed within tiny error (`lr_chunk_y_max=1e-8`, `lr_chunk_state_max=1.2e-7`, `lr_chunk_out_max=0.00022525`, `lr_layer_full_max=0.00108004`). Focused specs `spec/qwen35_delta_net_spec.cr spec/qwen35_forward_spec.cr` passed (`17 examples, 0 failures`). 27B was not rerun because no local `*27B*Q4_K_M*.gguf` was found under `~/.cache/lm-studio/models`.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 hostile component speed gate with the real 27B path (`/Users/sergey/.cache/lm-studio/models/lmstudio-community/Qwen3.6-27B-GGUF/Qwen3.6-27B-Q4_K_M.gguf`): added `--simulate-lowrank-metal-project` to isolate single-token Metal Q/K projection plus low-rank step. On 9B/27B layer2 rank64, `lowrank_delta_step` and `lowrank_project_coeffs + lowrank_delta_step` are accurate (`y_max<=1e-8`, state max `<=1.2e-7`) and faster than the CPU proof, but Metal projection adds little over CPU-coeff Metal step at single-token granularity (`9B 21.19ms -> 22.33ms`, `27B 24.08ms -> 25.27ms` for 32 steps). The real local accelerator is chunk residency: `lowrank_project_coeffs_chunk + lowrank_delta_chunk_step_parallel` runs 32 steps in `4.24ms` on 9B and `4.03ms` on 27B vs CPU proof `536.5ms`/`811.1ms`; chunk-out and full-layer known-span paths are also accurate and much faster than CPU proof (`27B full-layer 64-step reruns ~16-18ms Metal vs ~1040-1063ms CPU proof`, `layer_max=0.00179482`). However, current autoregressive self-draft is not yet faster than exact (`9B 188.9ms draft vs 180.1ms exact`, `27B 630.5ms draft vs 493.9ms exact`, agreement `8/8`), and wall self-spec with `layers=0,2,4` remains draft-bound despite 100% acceptance (`9B metal_project draft/verifier 2489.7/144.1ms`, speedup_est `1.0579x`; `27B 4298.3/360.5ms`, `1.0839x`). Fresh verifier LTP is strong (`32-token`: `4.34x` 9B, `5.28x` 27B), but cross-queue draft/verifier overlap remains weak (`N=48`: `1.0628x` 9B, `1.0547x` 27B). Conclusion: the kernels accelerate the known-span pieces; the missing speedup is still controller/dataflow residency, not low-rank math correctness.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 cheap-draft falsifier sweep: added probe-only `--simulate-cheap-self-draft-variants` with `lowrank-no-ffn`, `skip-layer`, and `early-exit-N`. This confirms why the prefill/LTP idea helps the verifier but not proposal generation by itself: known candidate spans can run chunk-major, while unknown draft tokens still require autoregressive top1 decisions. On 9B `layers=0,2,4`, `lowrank-no-ffn` kept `100%` acceptance for `gen=4` but only reduced draft from `~1296ms` to `~1257ms`; `skip-layer` fell to `40%` acceptance and got slower. On 27B `layers=0,2,4`, `lowrank-no-ffn` fell to `50%` acceptance and `skip-layer` to `0%`. Narrower no-FFN layer sets did not generalize: 27B `layers=0` and `0,2` still fell to `50%` acceptance. Naive early-exit is worse: 9B `early-exit-8/16` had `0%` acceptance and `early-exit-24` `33%`; 27B short `gen=2` had `0%` acceptance for `early-exit-8/16/24`. Verdict: cheap proposal needs either a trained/calibrated draft head or a different draft model/state objective; naive layer skipping/early-exit is not a safe production branch.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 progressive tree oracle probe: added `--simulate-self-spec-tree-k=K`, which models low-rank top-K candidates chosen before generating a wrong tail. It reports exact parity, top1/topK hit rates, rank-ordered branch checks, full top-K branch checks, and the same unit cost model used by self-spec. Easy rank64 9B/27B smokes were all top1 (`100%`, no branching), so they do not justify tree work by themselves. A deliberately hostile 27B rank8/code-SQL prompt produced the needed signal: ordinary long self-spec with `schedule=16/top5-rescue` had one rejection after paying for 20 verifier tokens (`75%` accept, estimated `0.9379x`), while the tree oracle caught the same miss in top5 before the tail (`top1=93.75%`, `top5=100%`, `branch_tokens_rank=17`, `branch_tokens_full=20`, `rank_speedup=1.1204x`, `full_speedup=0.9858x`). Verdict: tree-before-tail is mathematically real but only attractive if the verifier can avoid full topK cost or can batch it cheaply; next gate is a prompt suite at production ranks (`32/48/64`) and a real batched verifier tree cost.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 tiny oracle-head falsifier: added `--simulate-topk-oracle=K` and `--simulate-topk-oracle-train-tokens=N`. This is not a full LM-head; it trains only token/rank bias and a margin gate over low-rank draft topK, then tests on later exact-greedy steps. Result: simple bias/rank reranking did not improve the tested cases. On 9B rank64 it correctly chose no-op because top1 was already `100%`. On 27B rank8 hostile code/SQL (`gen=32/top5`), the miss is inside top5, but if train does not include a miss the margin gate stays off and test keeps `baseline_top1=95.83%`, `baseline_avg_branch=1.042`; if train includes the miss, the learned threshold (`0.4007`) does not overgate clean test and still no rerank gain. Rank16/32 on the same prompt were also test-clean (`100%` top1). Verdict: a tiny token/rank bias is too weak and data-sparse; a useful oracle-head likely needs a real prompt suite plus richer features (draft margin history, low-rank residuals, layer-set/rank signals), not a full vocab LM-head.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 FFN sparsity quality gate: added probe-only draft variants `lowrank-ffn-top-P`, keeping only the top-P percent by absolute `SwiGLU = silu(gate) * up` activation before `ffn_down` inside low-rank recurrent draft layers. This currently measures quality/acceptance only, not real speed, because `ffn_down` still runs dense and top-M sorting adds overhead. Separate `layers=0,2,4/rank64` smokes kept `100%` acceptance for `top40/top20/top10` on 9B (`gen=8`) and 27B (`gen=4`). Hybrid early recurrent layers `0,1,2,4,5,6/rank64` also kept `100%` acceptance for `top40/top20/top10` on 9B (`gen=4`) and `top40/top20` on 27B (`gen=2`). A 9B all-recurrent short smoke (`0,1,2,...,30` excluding full-attn cadence, `gen=2`) kept `100%` acceptance for `top40/top20`. Verdict: FFN activation sparsity is the most promising next math lever, but the next gate must implement or simulate sparse `ffn_down` cost and run a larger prompt suite; do not treat the current dense-zero wall timings as speed evidence.
  - [x] 2026-04-27 FFN activation PCA quality gate: added `lowrank-ffn-pca-R`, which collects calibration `SwiGLU = silu(gate) * up` activations per selected recurrent layer, builds a PCA basis, and projects draft FFN activations before `ffn_down`. With `tokens=64/calib=32/pca_iters=4`, `layers=0,2,4/rank64` kept `100%` acceptance for PCA ranks `16/32/64` on 9B (`gen=4`) and `16/32` on 27B (`gen=2`), matching top-M quality in these tiny smokes. Hybrid early recurrent layers `0,1,2,4,5,6/rank64` kept `100%` acceptance for PCA `16/32` on 9B (`gen=2`) and 27B (`gen=1`). Caveat: the baseline low-rank drift on early6 already shows teacher-forced top1 misses on the held-out prompt (`93.75%` 9B, `96.88%` 27B), so this is only a small generation acceptance signal. Verdict: activation PCA is at least as interesting as top-M and much more compact (`rank16/32` vs thousands of FFN channels), but next gate must add a real cost model/kernel for `B^T a` + `WdB`, plus a larger prompt suite.
- [x] 2026-04-27 FFN PCA-down factorization gate: added probe-only `lowrank-ffn-pca-down-R`, which precomputes `WdB = ffn_down * basis` per recurrent layer and replaces dense `ffn_down(PCA(a))` in the low-rank draft with `sum_j (B_j^T a) * WdB_j`. This checks the actual `ffn_down` cheapening formula instead of merely zeroing/projecting activations before a dense matmul. 9B `layers=0,2,4/rank8/tokens=64/calib=32/gen=4` matched dense PCA quality (`lowrank-ffn-pca-32` and `lowrank-ffn-pca-down-32` both `100%` acceptance, parity true, same exact/emitted ids). Initial 27B stress with `rank8/calib16/pca16/gen2` showed PCA-down adds no extra degradation relative to dense PCA, but PCA itself reduced acceptance versus baseline. Fairer 27B reruns with projected-DeltaNet `rank64` recovered clean short-smoke quality: default prompt `calib32/pca-down32/gen4`, default prompt `calib64/pca-down64/gen4`, and adversarial code/SQL prompt `calib32/pca-down32/gen4` all kept `100%` acceptance/parity and matched baseline exact/emitted ids. Verdict: `WdB` factorization is algebraically viable and compact, and PCA-down ranks `32/64` look healthy in short 27B smokes; current Crystal-loop timings are not production speed evidence.
- [x] 2026-04-27 global/sidecar FFN PCA-down smoke: added repeated `--ffn-pca-calib-prompt=TEXT` so FFN PCA/`WdB` bases can be built from external calibration prompts instead of the eval prompt. With three external calibration prompts (`72` FFN activation vectors per layer at `calib=24`), held-out Crystal/log parsing eval kept `100%` acceptance/parity for `layers=0,2,4`, projected-DeltaNet `rank64`, and `pca-down32` on both 9B (`gen=4`) and 27B (`gen=4`). A stronger 27B `gen=8` rank sweep also kept `100%` acceptance/parity for both `pca-down16` and `pca-down32`. Caveat: DeltaNet K-bases in this probe remain prompt-local; this specifically tests whether FFN PCA/`WdB` can be sidecar/global rather than prompt-local. Verdict: sidecar adapter is plausible enough for a larger 30-50 prompt held-out falsifier.
- [x] 2026-04-27 sidecar FFN PCA-down held-out falsifier: 9B external-basis 8-prompt `gen=8` suite completed without exactness failures in the visible summary; 27B external-basis 4-prompt `gen=8` suite preserved parity on all prompts but reduced acceptance versus baseline lowrank on 3/4 prompts. Baseline lowrank stayed `100%` accepted on all 27B prompts, while `pca-down16/32` dropped to `70%`, `45.45/54.55%`, `100%`, and `87.5%` acceptance. Rechecking three affected prompts with wider `tokens=88/calib=40` and `pca-down64` only partially helped (`77.78%`, `54.55%`, `70%`). The worst JSON/db-shards prompt also failed with prompt-local dense `lowrank-ffn-pca-32/64` at the same `45.45%` acceptance as `pca-down`, proving the issue is the FFN PCA projection, not the `WdB` factorization. Verdict: exact verifier makes this safe, but current FFN PCA sidecar is not production-ready as a high-acceptance cheap draft; next gates are more calibration data, layer/rank gating, and risk-aware fallback before any Metal kernel.
- [x] 2026-04-27 all-recurrent PCA-down stress: tested `lowrank-ffn-pca-down` across all 24 recurrent layers of 9B (`0,1,2,4,5,6,...,30`) on the JSON/db-shards prompt with external FFN PCA basis. Baseline all-recurrent lowrank kept `100%` acceptance. `pca-down16` collapsed immediately (`gen=2`, `33.33%` acceptance). `pca-down32` recovered on `gen=2` (`100%`) but fell on `gen=4` (`60%`, parity still true). Verdict: all-layer PCA-down is exactly safe but acceptance-fragile; do not run expensive 27B all-layer stress until layer/rank gating is designed. Speed potential is real because dense `ffn_down` can be replaced by `rank*(n_ff+n_embd)` work per layer (tens to >100x cheaper for that one matmul), but `gate/up` remain dense and rejection economics can erase the win.
- [x] 2026-04-27 even/odd recurrent PCA-down split: on the JSON/db-shards adversary prompt with external FFN PCA basis, 9B `pca-down32` over even recurrent layers (`0,2,4,...,30`) and odd recurrent layers (`1,5,9,...,29`) both kept `100%` acceptance at `gen=4`, while the prior all-recurrent run fell at `gen=4`. At `gen=8`, even recurrent collapsed to `18.75%` acceptance, while odd recurrent held `77.78%`; increasing odd to `pca-down64` did not improve acceptance. A 27B `gen=4` smoke showed the same direction: even recurrent `60%`, odd recurrent `75%`, baseline lowrank `100%` in both. Early-even was clean in the 9B `gen=4` smoke, late-even dropped to `75%`. Verdict: odd recurrent is less toxic than even, and late-even likely contains fragile layers; parity remains safe, but the next useful sweep is layer buckets (`early/mid/late`, modulo full-attn blocks), not raw all-even/all-odd.
- [x] 2026-04-27 mod/depth bucket PCA-down sweep: on 9B JSON/db-shards `gen=8`, single `mod4` buckets showed `mod1` is least toxic (`77.78%` acceptance), while `mod0` and `mod2` were worse (`54.55%` each); combining two mod buckets was much worse (`10-18.75%`). After a too-large batch was interrupted, smaller one-bucket reruns showed all depth buckets individually pass at `gen=4`: `early_0_10`, `mid_12_22`, and `late_24_30` all kept `100%` acceptance/parity with `pca-down32`. PCA component sign/handedness is unlikely to be the direct cause because flipping a PCA vector sign leaves `(B^T a) * (W_down B)` invariant if both `B` and `WdB` are flipped consistently. The suspicious pattern is more likely phase/depth interaction: PCA errors from separate recurrent cadence buckets are tolerable alone but compound when combined. Next gate: pairwise depth bucket combinations and per-layer leave-one-out within bad combinations.
- [x] 2026-04-27 depth-pair PCA-down narrowing: pairwise depth buckets on 9B JSON/db-shards `gen=4` showed `early+mid` drops to `60%`, `mid+late` to `75%`, while `early+late` stays `100%`; however, `early+late` falls to `18.75%` at `gen=8`, so `gen=4` is too weak as a gate. Coarse leave-one-out inside `early+mid` found `early+mid_mod1` is clean (`100%`) while `early+mid_mod0` and `early+mid_mod2` both drop to `60%`. Single-depth `gen=8` reruns then showed the real stable candidate is `late_24_30`: 9B `late_24_30` stayed `100%`, while `early_0_10` fell to `25%` and `mid_12_22` to `41.67%`. A 27B `late_24_30/gen=8` smoke also kept `100%` acceptance/parity with `pca-down32`. Verdict: late recurrent bucket (`24,25,26,28,29,30`) is the first plausible stable FFN PCA-down sidecar target; early/mid need deeper gating or fallback.
- [x] 2026-04-27 27B 64-layer depth correction: the 9B-stable `late_24_30` does not mean "absolute tail" on 27B. Testing true 27B deeper buckets on JSON/db-shards showed broad late buckets are fragile: `mid_to_end_32_62` and `last_third_42_62` both fell to `20%` acceptance with `pca-down32`; narrower `last_quarter_48_62` also fell to `20%`; final tail `56,57,58,60,61,62` improved only to `40%`. Increasing the final tail to `pca-down64` did not improve acceptance (`40%`). Baseline lowrank stayed `100%` and parity stayed true throughout. Verdict: for 27B, stable PCA-down buckets are not simply "last third/end"; the promising band may be around layers `24..30` or another mid-depth island, so the next 27B sweep should scan narrow 6-layer recurrent bands (`24..30`, `32..38`, `40..46`, `48..54`, `56..62`) instead of huge suffixes.
- [x] 2026-04-27 27B narrow-band PCA-down scan: on JSON/db-shards `gen=4`, narrow 6-layer recurrent bands show a clear mid-depth island. `24..30` and `32..38` both kept `100%` acceptance/parity with `pca-down32`; `40..46` and `48..54` dropped to `75%`; `56..62` dropped to `40%`. Combining individually clean bands was not safe: `24..38` dropped to `60%`, and `24..46` also stayed at `60%`. Baseline lowrank remained `100%` everywhere. Verdict: the first 27B sidecar candidates are single narrow bands `24..30` or `32..38`, not broad suffixes and not combined islands; PCA error budget appears non-additive across bands.
- [x] 2026-04-27 PCA up/down falsifier: added `lowrank-ffn-pca-updown-R`, which trains a small ridge adapter from `ffn_in` directly to PCA activation coefficients and then applies precomputed `WdB`, bypassing dense `gate/up`, `SwiGLU`, and dense `ffn_down` in the draft FFN path. This is the first probe for cheapening the whole FFN sub-block, not only `ffn_down`. On the JSON/db-shards prompt, stable band `24,25,26,28,29,30` kept `100%` acceptance/parity on 9B (`gen=8`, `pca-updown32`) and 27B (`gen=4`, `pca-updown32`). Caveat: current timings are Crystal-loop proof timings and not Metal speed claims; the quality signal is the main result. Verdict: PCA up/down is surprisingly viable on the stable band and deserves a multi-prompt suite plus a Metal cost model.
- [x] 2026-04-27 PCA up/down held-out suite: 9B `late_24_30` (`24,25,26,28,29,30`) passed a 3-prompt external-calibration suite at `gen=8` with `100%` acceptance/parity for `lowrank`, `pca-down32`, `pca-updown16`, and `pca-updown32`. 27B single-band suites on `24..30` and `32..38` each passed parity on all 3 prompts with no exactness failures; baseline lowrank stayed `100%`, while `pca-down32`, `pca-updown16`, and `pca-updown32` all matched each other at `91.67%` average acceptance with one rejection total per band. Verdict: `pca-updown16` is not weaker than `pca-updown32` in this limited suite and can be the first Metal cost target, but 27B still has a rejection tax; next gate should run `gen=16` and a larger hostile prompt suite before production kernels.
- [x] 2026-04-27 PCA up/down `gen=16` gate: parity stayed true everywhere, but acceptance split by model/band. 9B `late_24_30` over 4 hostile prompts fell sharply: baseline lowrank stayed `100%`, while `pca-down32` averaged `74.45%`, `pca-updown16` `63.45%`, and `pca-updown32` `60.79%` acceptance. 27B was better but band-sensitive: `band_24_30` had `pca-updown32` at `100%` average acceptance (`0` rejections across 3 prompts), while `pca-down32` averaged `96.08%` and `pca-updown16` `92.98%`; `band_32_38` was weaker (`pca-updown32` `83.39%`, `pca-updown16` `89.39%`). Verdict: do not promote `pca-updown` as a production sidecar yet. Kernel work is still justified as an isolated cost/proof microkernel, with first target `27B band_24_30 pca-updown32`, because that is the only `gen=16` configuration that survived cleanly.
- [x] 2026-04-27 PCA up/down Metal microkernel gate: added probe kernels for `ffn_in -> coeffs -> WdB`, including a fused single-dispatch `ffn_pca_updown_fused` path and `Qwen35Metal.ffn_pca_updown_out_resident`. Release smokes are numerically clean: 27B layer24 rank32 hidden5120 `max_delta=2.4e-7`, `rmse=4.0e-8`; 9B layer24 rank32 hidden4096 `max_delta=2.03e-6`, `rmse=1.8e-7`. Standalone timing is not a win by itself (`27B` CPU adapter proof `0.3495ms` vs Metal `1.1456ms`, `9B` `0.2397ms` vs `1.5673ms`, with resident adapter but per-call `ffn_in` upload + wait + readback). Verdict: correctness is proven, but speed requires integration into the existing low-rank layer command buffer with no readback and a comparison against the dense FFN tail it replaces; do not ship it as a separate per-layer dispatch.
- [x] 2026-04-27 Integrated PCA up/down layer-kernel gate: added token-row kernel `ffn_pca_updown_fused_rows`, wired it into `lowrank_delta_chunk_projected_layer_async` as an optional FFN tail replacement, and added `--simulate-lowrank-metal-layer-updown=R`. This keeps `normed -> pca-updown -> residual add` inside the same low-rank layer command buffer. Combined release runs on layer24/rank64 DeltaNet + rank32 updown over a 40-token held-out chunk show a real Metal speed gate versus the dense full-layer FFN path: 27B dense full-layer `18.036ms` vs integrated updown `9.134ms` (`~1.97x`) with `max=0.00045776`; 9B dense full-layer `16.494ms` vs integrated updown `10.904ms` (`~1.51x`) with `max=0.00292206`. Verdict: the kernel shape is now promising; next step is not more standalone microbenching, but wiring sidecar resident buffers per selected layer and testing self-draft wall acceptance/speed on the 27B `24..30` band.
- [x] 2026-04-27 Whole-chain wall baseline before wiring integrated updown: 27B `layers=24,25,26,28,29,30`, external FFN calibration, `gen=8`, schedule `4,4`, `metal_project=1`, JSON/db-shards prompt preserved `100%` acceptance/parity. Current wall remains draft-bound: baseline `lowrank` reports `draft_ms=1070.831`, `verifier_ms=363.560`, `speedup_est=1.3395x`; probe-side `lowrank-ffn-pca-updown-32` reports `draft_ms=1011.050`, `verifier_ms=351.098`, `speedup_est=1.3473x`. Verdict: quality is good, but the existing wall path still uses CPU-orchestrated per-token draft and does not call the integrated layer-updown kernel; more local PCA kernel work is premature. Next useful implementation is to route selected recurrent draft layers through the integrated updown layer kernel or otherwise remove CPU orchestration/LM-head bottlenecks, then rerun this same wall gate.
- [x] 2026-04-27 Routed integrated PCA up/down into the actual wall self-draft path behind `--simulate-self-spec-wall-metal-layer-updown`. `LowRankState` now caches resident updown sidecar buffers, and `lowrank-ffn-pca-updown-R` can call the integrated Metal layer-updown route for selected recurrent draft layers. The gate is negative at the current single-token orchestration granularity: on 9B `layer24/gen4`, routed updown kept parity but was slower than lowrank baseline (`draft_ms=175.732` vs `165.920`); on 27B `layers=24,25,26,28,29,30/gen8/schedule=4,4`, routed `pca-updown32` kept parity but had `70.0%` acceptance and `draft_ms=1524.354`, essentially tied with the CPU-side updown comparison (`1519.603`) and worse than baseline lowrank (`~1150-1219`). Verdict: the integrated kernel is still useful for known-span/layer-major chunks, but wiring it as one layer dispatch/readback per autoregressive token does not accelerate self-draft. Next useful implementation must keep multiple layers/tokens GPU-resident through the draft lane and read back only logits/top1, not layer outputs.
- [x] 2026-04-27 LTP/WBA orchestration deep dive found the missing GPU-resident link for block self-draft: `forward_decode_wave_async` still starts from CPU-dequantized `emb : Array(Float32)`, so a GPU `top1_id_buf` could not feed the next draft step without CPU `embedding_lookup`. Added `embed_q4k_f32_from_token_id` plus `Qwen35Metal.embedding_q4k_from_token_id[_buf]` for Q4_K token embeddings. Accuracy is exact against CPU dequant on both local targets (`9B token 271`, hidden4096, `max=0`, `rmse=0`; `27B token 20797`, hidden5120, `max=0`, `rmse=0`). The normal self-draft Metal smoke still passes (`steps=2`, agreement `2/2`). Verdict: `top1_id_buf -> next hidden_buf` is now technically possible on GPU; next implementation should refactor decode-wave into an appendable encoder stage so one `draft_cb` can chain `gamma` token steps before any CPU readback.

This removes intermediate CPU token readback/CPU embedding lookup, though it still uses one command buffer per token rather than one appendable block command buffer. Correctness held: 9B `layers=24/rank64/N=4` matched exact greedy `4/4` with ids `4475,71590,440,20797`; WBA showed submits were front-loaded (`submit_ms=13.150`) and execution/readback dominated (`wait_ms=71.603`, `chain_ms=84.752`, exact `86.018`). 27B `N=2` also matched exact `2/2` (`chain_ms=123.437`, exact `122.135`). Verdict: GPU `top1 -> embedding -> next top1` chaining is now proven, but not yet faster; next cut is to collapse the per-token command buffers into an appendable `draft_cb` and then overlap `draft_cb[k+1]` with verifier `cb[k]`.
