---

## Correctness criterion (all phases)

Per-token cosine similarity vs llama.cpp HEAD on standard test prompt:
- Prefill 64 tokens from `"The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. Describe this scene in detail:"`-style prompt
- Decode 64 tokens
- Target: cosine ≥ 0.9999 per logit vector

## Speed criterion

Wall-clock tok/s measured with `/usr/bin/time`:
- Prefill tok/s (full 64 tokens)
- Decode tok/s (averaged over 64 tokens)
- KV save/load round-trip time
- Baseline: llama.cpp HEAD with identical settings (no-flash-attention default, as in previous bench)

## Status

- **Active phase:** 4 (optimization: beat llama.cpp)
- **Active task:** 4.9 (true layerwise/microbatch prefill)
- **Baseline (llama.cpp 86db42e97):** 9B Q4_K_M pp64=458 / tg64=43.5 tok/s (FA=0) → targets ≥504 / ≥48
- **Latest guarded benchmark:** prompt64/gen64 reps=5/warmup=2 with `--require-quiet --wait-quiet-ms=60000`: native prefill `426.01 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `461.90 tok/s` (`-7.77%`), native decode `47.60 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `45.35 tok/s` (`+4.96%`)
- **Latest relaxed-load benchmark after shared-H16 recurrent projection cleanup:** prompt64/gen64 reps=3/warmup=1: first-run native prefill `426.79 tok/s` p50 vs llama.cpp `459.43 tok/s` (`-7.10%`), preallocated-state prefill `448.60 tok/s` vs llama.cpp `465.91 tok/s` (`-3.71%`), prompt-cache restore `1350.65 tok/s` vs llama.cpp `465.80 tok/s` (`+189.97%`), and native decode remains ahead by `+4.05..4.47%`.
- **Latest self-spec kernel finding:** real GPU pipeline now has resident FFN `pca-updown` draft routing via `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown=R` and in-process A/B via `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updowns=LIST` (`0` = lowrank baseline). On Qwen3.6-27B, layers `24,25,26,28,29,30`, rank64, `gen=16`, schedule `4,4,8`, `pca-updown16` preserved `parity=true`/`100%` accept and improved `overlap_ms` from `2353.995` to `1818.438` (`~22.8%` relative) versus baseline lowrank, but still measured only `plain_speedup=0.5616x`; this is a useful draft-cost/acceptance improvement, not yet a direct plain-decode win.
- **Latest external-calibration falsifier:** with three external FFN calibration prompts on the same 27B band and `gen=16/schedule=4,4,8`, `pca-updown16` kept parity on 3/3 eval prompts and improved overlap on default/code, but did not robustly improve acceptance: default `83.33% -> 100%`, code `78.95% -> 78.95%`, JSON/db-shards `78.95% -> 68.42%`. Rechecking JSON with `pca-updown32` kept the same `68.42%` acceptance. Treat prompt-local pca-updown as promising, but external/global sidecar needs prompt-risk fallback or better calibration before production.
- **Latest risk-controller finding:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-fallback-on-reject`, which starts with `pca-updown` but disables it for future draft blocks after the first verifier rejection. On the external-calibration JSON/db-shards falsifier, fallback-on-reject preserved parity and improved `pca-updown16` from `68.42%` to `93.75%` acceptance, with `overlap_ms=1855.882` versus lowrank baseline `2197.082`; this is the first practical guard that makes the pca-updown speed lever less brittle.
- **Latest suite-harness finding:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-suite-prompt=NAME::TEXT` so one process can reuse loaded weights and external FFN adapters while rebuilding prompt-local K bases for additional eval prompts. The harness fail-closes when pca-updown suite runs without external `--ffn-pca-calib-prompt`, avoiding accidental reuse of main-prompt FFN adapters as if they were global. A 9B smoke with main+suite prompts, baseline vs `pca-updown16`, fallback-on-reject, and external calibration preserved parity across all suite rows.
- **Latest 27B suite falsifier:** in one-process Qwen3.6-27B external-calibrated suite (`main`, `code_fibonacci`, `json_db_shards`; `gen=16`, schedule `4,4,8`, layers `24,25,26,28,29,30`), `pca-updown16+fallback` preserved parity in all rows but was not a universal win: main improved overlap `2316.299 -> 2160.379` at same `83.33%` acceptance, code regressed `2055.370 -> 2619.448` and `83.33% -> 66.67%`, JSON improved acceptance `78.95% -> 93.75%` but worsened overlap `2916.710 -> 3336.802`. Next controller must be predictive/prompt-aware or schedule-aware; first-reject fallback alone is not enough.
- **Latest schedule-aware controller:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-after-full-accepts=N` and `draft_updown_chunks` reporting. The pca-updown draft route now can start disabled, enable only after N consecutive full-accept chunks, and reset/disable on any reject. Because the overlap scheduler pre-submits the next block before verification, enabling can lag by one block; a 9B `gen=6/schedule=2,2,2` smoke confirmed the lagged enable path with `draft_updown_chunks=1`, `accept_rate=100%`, and `parity=true`.
- **Latest warmup falsifier:** the same 27B 3-prompt suite with `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-after-full-accepts=1` preserved parity but did not beat baseline. Warmup used exactly two pca-updown chunks per prompt and regressed main acceptance/overlap (`83.33%/2293.430 -> 73.68%/2328.482`), code acceptance/overlap (`83.33%/2051.861 -> 78.95%/2128.309`), and JSON overlap (`2893.256 -> 2944.803` at same `78.95%`). Streak-only warmup is not enough; next pca-updown controller needs a predictive risk signal before enabling or should be deprioritized behind other exact levers.
- **Blocked:** nothing
- **Latest self-draft attribution finding:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-attribution`, which reports draft submit/commit/wait/read, low-rank projection, verifier initial target, verifier chunks, and final-tail skip counts. 9B `layers=0,2,4/rank64/gamma4/gen8` preserved parity but stayed behind plain exact (`plain_exact_ms ~183.8-189.3`, `overlap_ms ~294.9-304.0`); attribution showed draft wait dominates (`~96.8ms` wait vs `~10.2ms` submit for split=1) and verifier total remains `~180ms`. 27B `gamma4/gen4` also preserved parity but measured `plain_exact_ms=263.080`, `overlap_ms=464.075`, with draft wait `174.123ms`, verifier initial `104.881ms`, and verifier chunk `139.968ms`.
- **Latest self-draft head falsifier:** added `--simulate-self-draft-gpu-state-only=N`, a known-token lower-bound ablation that runs low-rank draft state updates without `emit_head`/top1. On 9B `layers=0,2,4/rank64/N=8`, normal GPU self-draft chain was `175.221ms` with `8/8` agreement while state-only/no-head was still `171.839ms`; on 27B `N=4`, normal chain was `239.326ms` and state-only/no-head was `286.895ms` under the current append-block shape. Verdict: LM-head/top1 is not the dominant wall. The draft body is still too close to the full model because only a small selected layer set is approximated; the next breakthrough target is a genuinely cheaper draft body via broader stable low-rank/PCA-updown coverage, layer-band routing, or a block-native cheap candidate model.
- **Latest hybrid self-draft finding:** added layer-local GPU draft route masks so one WBA decode-wave can mix baseline lowrank, selected-layer FFN skip, and selected-layer resident `pca-updown` without host-side branching. New knobs are `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-no-ffn-layers=LIST` and `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-layers=LIST`. 9B smoke `layers=0,2,4,24,25`, `no_ffn=0,2`, `updown16=24,25`, `gen=6/gamma2` preserved `parity=true`/`100%` accept and was a small/noisy win (`overlap_ms 234.821 pure -> 233.728 no-FFN -> 230.242 hybrid`). 27B smoke `layers=0,2,24,25`, `no_ffn=0`, `updown16=24,25`, `gen=4/gamma2` preserved parity/accept and improved overlap `455.947 -> 437.934` versus no-FFN-only. Verdict: hybrid routing is now technically enabled and mildly positive, but not yet a breakthrough; the next search should scan layer-mode assignments under attribution, not add more host-side controllers.
- **Latest timing-audit finding:** fixed `simulate_self_spec_gpu_pipeline_run` timing semantics. `overlap_ms` is now the real self-spec wall-clock from seed draft start to generation completion, before `plain_exact` and paired serial reruns. The old phase-sum undercounted reject paths by excluding replay/controller work, so any route with rejections could look better than it was. The probe output now also prints `serial_ms` explicitly; `speedup` is paired speculative-serial vs overlapped self-spec, while `plain_speedup` is the honest comparison against ordinary exact greedy decode. Smoke after the fix: 9B `layers=0,2,24,25`, `rank32`, `gamma2`, `gen4` preserved parity; pure lowrank had `plain_exact_ms=94.401`, `serial_ms=341.271`, `overlap_ms=164.454`, `plain_speedup=0.574x`; no-FFN routes with one reject now report `overlap_ms=266-270ms` instead of hiding the `~37ms` replay tax.
- **Latest paired-serial boundary audit:** fixed a second timing mismatch. `serial_ms` no longer reruns `verifier_state_after_prefix` inside the timed region, and `plain_exact_ms` no longer includes the state fork; both now use the same prepared-prompt boundary as `overlap_ms`. This invalidates older paired-serial `speedup` ratios as inflated. Corrected smokes: normal 9B `layers=0,2`, `rank16`, `gamma2`, `gen2` kept parity with `plain_exact_ms=43.649`, `serial_ms=95.498`, `overlap_ms=101.958`, `speedup=0.9366x`, `plain_speedup=0.4281x`; hybrid 9B `layers=0,2,24,25`, `rank32`, `gamma2`, `gen4` kept parity with pure lowrank `serial_ms=182.217`, `overlap_ms=165.699`, `speedup=1.0997x`, while reject/no-FFN routes dropped below paired serial (`speedup=0.9289..0.9362x`). Verdict: `plain_speedup` remains the only user-facing "faster than exact decode" metric; `speedup` is just an internal overlap-efficiency diagnostic.
- **Latest corrected self-spec gate:** after both timing-boundary fixes, the current same-weight GPU self-draft is exact but still not a direct decode speed win. 9B `layers=0,2,4`, `rank64`, `gamma4`, `gen8` kept `100%` accept/parity, but measured `plain_exact_ms=193.150`, `serial_ms=348.292`, `overlap_ms=320.052`, `speedup=1.0882x`, `plain_speedup=0.6035x`; attribution still shows draft wait as the largest draft component (`attr_draft_wait_block_ms=80.863`). 27B `layers=0,2,4`, `rank48`, `gamma4`, `gen4` also kept `100%` accept/parity, but measured `plain_exact_ms=252.121`, `serial_ms=557.085`, `overlap_ms=480.146`, `speedup=1.1602x`, `plain_speedup=0.5251x`, with `attr_draft_wait_block_ms=165.905`. Verdict: current self-draft is useful as exact overlap infrastructure, but the breakthrough still requires a much cheaper draft body or a different candidate source; more controller tuning alone will not beat plain exact decode.
- **Latest exact candidate-source pivot:** defaulted `QWEN35_DECODE_POLICY=auto` to the already-verified exact n-gram risk gate and large-chunk staging (`stage_min=16`, `risk_gate=true`) while preserving explicit `ngram` as the manual old-default path. This is fail-closed: risky n-gram chunks are skipped and target greedy decode remains exact. 27B+0.8B harness repeat smoke (`tokens=8`, first 4 repeat prompts) measured `ngram_router16_staged_risk` at `41.61ms/tok` vs plain `56.76ms/tok` (`1.364x`) and beat default neural speculative on all 4 prompts. CLI smoke on 9B showed auto keeps good repeats fast (`alpha`, `13/13` accepted, `12.40ms/tok` vs old `11.93ms/tok`) and avoids the bad YAML repeat chunk (`risk_auto`: 0 n-gram candidates, `21.20ms/tok`; old no-risk: `5/16` accepted, `27.26ms/tok`). A follow-up CLI final-tail skip avoids computing an unused target next-token after the last requested output token; after that fix, no-repeat auto was near greedy (`def fibonacci`: `303.2ms` auto vs `304.1ms` greedy; sky prompt: `306.5ms` vs `305.0ms`), good repeat alpha measured `187.6ms` (`11.73ms/tok`) versus greedy `303.8ms`, and YAML risk auto matched greedy token ids with essentially equal wall (`309.9ms` auto vs `309.1ms` greedy).
- **Latest speculative harness tail audit:** `qwen35_speculative_accept` now uses the same final-tail semantics as the CLI. Greedy baseline, n-gram verifier chunks, neural staged/chunk/serial verifiers, and draft proposal loops avoid computing logits for the unused next-token after the final requested generated token. The shared `target_prefill_top1s_for_future` helper also truncates verifier chunks when candidate stages are longer than the remaining generation budget. Verification: release build `/tmp/qwen35_spec_accept_tail`; exact 27B+0.8B n-gram smoke accepted `8/8` with generated text matching target greedy (`spec_wall=290.6ms`, `plain_target_wall=394.8ms`); first-four-repeat sweep measured `ngram_router16_staged_risk` at `36.52ms/tok` vs plain `49.62ms/tok` (`1.359x`); `crystal spec spec/ngram_draft_spec.cr` passed (`11 examples`).
- **Latest n-gram staging pivot:** WBA on the remaining n-gram verifier cost showed the bottleneck is the full FFN-heavy prefill body, not head/tail overhead. The old `stage_min=16` split a 16-token repeat into four small verifier chunks, paying that body repeatedly. N-gram risk gating is now decoupled from staging through `QWEN35_NGRAM_RISK_MIN_SIZE` / `QWEN35_SPEC_NGRAM_RISK_MIN_SIZE` (default `16`), and `auto` / speculative harness defaults keep common n-gram chunks whole by setting default stage-min to `ngram_gamma + 1`. Verification on 27B+0.8B repeat `tokens=16`: new `ngram_router16_risk` averaged `30.61ms/tok` vs staged risk `43.59ms/tok` and plain `53.75ms/tok` over 8 repeat prompts; good 16-token repeats are now about `20.6-20.9ms/tok` (`~2.5x` vs plain), while YAML/JSON risk prompts still fail closed to target-only. CLI auto on 9B alpha improved from old staged `14.85ms/tok` to bulk `8.37ms/tok`, with greedy-vs-auto token parity confirmed on alpha and YAML.
- **Latest debug-path fix:** `prefill_tokens_top1s` now has a correct serial verifier fallback when `QWEN35_PREFILL_CHUNK_OFF=1`; before this, multi-token verifier spans could crash with `Buffer size must be positive` because the chunk-off hidden helper only returned the final row. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_spec_accept_chunkoff`, reproduced the chunk-off n-gram smoke without crash (`accepted=4/4`), and `crystal spec spec/ngram_draft_spec.cr` still passes.
- **Latest n-gram CLI-default fix:** `bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr` now recomputes default `ngram_stage_min = ngram_gamma + 1` after CLI parsing unless `QWEN35_SPEC_NGRAM_STAGE_MIN` or `--ngram-stage-min` was explicit. This prevents `--ngram-gamma N` from leaving a stale pre-parse default. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_spec_accept_stage_default`; smoke with `--ngram-gamma 4` printed `ngram_stage_min=5`, kept `accepted=4/4`, and generated the expected repeat.
- **Latest n-gram rollback experiment:** added exact opt-in replay-on-reject for n-gram chunks (`QWEN35_NGRAM_REPLAY_ON_REJECT=1` in the CLI and `QWEN35_SPEC_NGRAM_REPLAY_ON_REJECT=1` / `--ngram-replay-on-reject` in the harness). The mode skips target-state backup copies before n-gram verifier chunks and rebuilds the exact target state from `prompt + generated_ids` only after a non-final reject. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_spec_accept_replay` and `/tmp/qwen35_generate_replay`; 27B+0.8B alpha repeat improved `21.73 -> 20.39ms/tok` with `100%` accept and `target_backup=0`; repeat8 risk-gated suite improved `30.85 -> 30.21ms/tok`; forced no-risk YAML reject regressed `71.53 -> 95.86ms/tok`, so the mode remains opt-in. 9B CLI auto alpha with replay matched greedy token ids and measured `7.91ms/tok`; YAML auto stayed fail-closed with `cycles=0` and `target_replay_ms=0.0`.
- **Latest self-draft search tooling:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-hybrid-rich-sweep`, an opt-in richer layer-mode sweep for the GPU-resident same-weight self-draft route. It extends the existing hybrid sweep with per-layer `no-FFN` / `pca-updown`, prefix/suffix bands up to 4 layers, and alternating no-FFN/updown assignments. This is search infrastructure for the next super-fusion pass: find stable layer maps first, then fuse the winning route. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_self_spec_rich_sweep_probe`; a 9B smoke over layers `0,2,4`, rank16, `gamma=2`, `gen=2`, `pca-updown16`, and attribution preserved parity on all emitted rich routes. Best smoke rows were `updown_single_0` (`plain_speedup=0.5203x`, `100%` accept) and `updown_single_2` (`0.5113x`, `100%` accept); mixed no-FFN/updown routes often rejected, confirming the search is useful and that no-FFN needs tight layer gating.
- **Latest cost truth-table probe:** added `--simulate-cost-truth-table=LIST` with optional `--simulate-cost-truth-updown=R` / `--simulate-cost-truth-updown-layers=LIST`. It prints normalized rows for serial exact decode, exact chunk-major verifier, low-rank known-token state-only lower bound, low-rank GPU top1 chain, and PCA-updown variants. A 9B `layers=0,2,4/rank64/chunks=1,2,4,8,16/updown16` run showed the verifier hypothesis is real for known spans (`k16=8.021ms/tok` vs serial `21.679ms/tok`), but the current same-weight draft body is the limiter: low-rank GPU chain stayed near serial cost with `16/16` agreement, while PCA-updown was only slightly cheaper and broke free-run agreement (`1/16`). Verdict: next speed work should make proposal generation cheaper without losing agreement; verifier LTP is not the primary blocker at accepted chunk sizes.
- **Latest route-scoreboard tooling:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-route-scoreboard`, and rich hybrid sweeps print the scoreboard automatically. The scoreboard hard-gates parity, ranks routes by same-run pure-lowrank baseline improvement when a baseline exists, and still prints `plain_speedup`, `accept%`, `overlap_ms`, `draft_wait_ms`, `replay_ms`, rejections, and `baseline_delta%` (`--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updowns=0,R`). Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_self_spec_route_score_probe`; a 9B smoke with layers `0,2`, rank8, `gamma=2`, `gen=2`, `pca-updown8`, and `draft-updowns=0,8` emitted `self_spec_route_scoreboard rows=9 baselines=1`, with all printed rows preserving parity and `100%` acceptance. The smoke is tooling evidence only; route ranking needs longer prompt sweeps before being treated as a performance result.
- **Latest suite prompt file tooling:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-suite-prompts-file=PATH` for loading additional suite prompts from a text file. Each non-empty non-comment line is either `NAME::TEXT` or raw `TEXT`, and both CLI and file prompts share the same name sanitizer. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_suite_file_probe`; smoke file added `code` and `json` suite rows and printed aggregate/per-layer route features for both; `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` passed.
- **Latest risk-offramp probe:** added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-risk-offramp-margin=F`, which uses draft top1/top2 margin to avoid pre-submitting the next draft block before exact verification. The implementation preserves parity and reports `risk_offramp_*` counters, but the first long-reasoning gate refutes raw margin as the current confidence signal: with 9B `gen=16`, schedule `4,4,8`, layers `0,2,4,6,8,10`, rank48, `tree2_first`, threshold `0.1` delayed one accepted next block and regressed wall `767.703ms -> 856.971ms`; the actual first-token reject was already handled by `tree2_first`. Conclusion: direct exact fallback on known first-token mismatch is already present; the next useful guard needs a better route-specific confidence signal than raw margin alone.
- **Latest route-oracle scoreboard:** added `self_spec_route_oracle` after suite hybrid sweeps. It groups rows by prompt/mode/split, compares each parity-preserving route against the pure lowrank baseline, and prints the per-prompt best route plus total oracle delta. The first real 4-prompt 9B suite (`main/code/json/reason`, `gen=16`, schedule `4,4,8`, layers `0,2,4,6,8,10`, rank48, `tree2_first`) showed a useful dynamic-selection upper bound: static `noffn_0_2` is not safe as a default because code regressed (`745.188ms pure -> 959.650ms`, two rejects), but per-prompt oracle selected `noffn_0_2` for main/reason, `pure` or `noffn_0` for code/json, and reduced total overlap by about `13%` versus pure. This keeps dynamic route selection alive while refuting static route promotion.
- **Latest timing-boundary fix:** normal self-spec reject paths no longer replay/rebuild exact state after a final-token reject. `overlap_ms` was already measured as real wall from seed draft start to generation-loop completion, but the normal reject branch could still compute an unused next-token target after the last requested emitted token. Now replay/rebuild/resync only run when `emitted_tokens < gen_tokens`; paired-serial diagnostic path matches this boundary. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_tail_reject_probe`; forced final reject smoke (`gen_tokens=1`, rank8/layers `0,2`) preserved parity and reported `replay_ms=0.0`, `attr_draft_resyncs=0`; top2 spec passed.
- **Latest 9B rich-route sweep:** first bounded real sweep used layers `0,2,4,6,8,10`, rank48, `gamma=4`, `gen=8`, `draft-updowns=0,16,32`, attribution, and route scoreboard. It emitted `self_spec_route_scoreboard rows=64 baselines=1`. The best default-prompt candidates were `noffn_prefix4` (`no_ffn=0,2,4,6`, `overlap_ms=303.750`, `baseline_delta=10.31%`, `plain_speedup=0.6294x`, `accept=100%`, `parity=true`), `hybrid_prefix3_suffix2` with updown16 (`304.886`, `9.97%`, `100%` accept), and `noffn_0_2` (`305.776`, `9.71%`, `100%` accept). Pure low-rank baseline was `overlap_ms=338.661`, `plain_speedup=0.5760x`. Verdict: early-prefix no-FFN is the current best fusion candidate on this prompt, but still slower than ordinary exact decode; next gate is prompt stability and 27B.
- **Latest prompt-stability gate:** aggressive `noffn_prefix4` (`0,2,4,6`) is not stable enough: default/json kept `100%` accept/parity, but the code prompt had one reject (`accept=77.78%`, replay `57.378ms`, `overlap_ms=517.678`). The less aggressive `noffn_0_2` route passed default/code/json with `100%` accept and parity (`overlap_ms` `323.575/327.117/314.289`). Verdict: `noffn_0_2` is the current stable 9B candidate; `noffn_prefix4` is a default-prompt optimization trap unless a confidence/reject predictor gates it.
- **Latest 27B bounded sweep:** 27B mid-band `24,25,26,28,29,30`, rank48, `gamma=4`, `gen=4`, `draft-updowns=0,16` emitted `self_spec_route_scoreboard rows=38 baselines=1`. All tested routes preserved parity and `100%` accept; best was suffix no-FFN (`noffn_suffix3=28,29,30`) with only `3.23%` overlap improvement (`463.777ms` vs pure `479.258ms`, `plain_speedup=0.5669x`). This is a one-chunk screen, so it is not enough for final scheduler conclusions; next 27B gate should be focused two-chunk A/B (`gen=8`) on pure vs suffix no-FFN rather than another broad rich sweep.
- **Latest 27B two-chunk A/B:** focused `gen=8/gamma=4` A/B confirmed the 27B mid-band route is only a small win. Pure low-rank: `overlap_ms=896.568`, `plain_speedup=0.5788x`, `accept=100%`, `parity=true`. `noffn_suffix3=28,29,30`: `overlap_ms=873.904`, `plain_speedup=0.5945x`, `accept=100%`, `parity=true`, roughly `2.53%` faster than pure. Verdict: suffix no-FFN is safe on this prompt but not a breakthrough; 27B needs either a wider stable cheap route, a focused super-fusion that removes dispatch/submit cost, or a different candidate-source trick.
- **Latest tree/state-pool finding:** corrected `Qwen35CPU.copy_state_metal_used!` / `encode_state_metal_used_copy!` to take explicit `used_tokens`. The earlier position-based helper was refuted by a strengthened spec: current full-attention `LayerState.position` is reserved/unused, so copying by layer position silently skipped live KV and could diverge on subsequent exact decode. The fixed helper copies recurrent DeltaNet state in full and full-attention KV only up to the caller-provided live token count. Updated microbench smoke `/tmp/qwen35_branch_state_copy_live_used_tokens_smoke.log` keeps state copy off the tree-spec blocker list: at `pos64`, two branch copies cost `0.912ms` on 9B and `2.367ms` on 27B via blit-used, versus old full-state memcpy `7.455ms` / `16.769ms`; eight 27B branches at `pos64` cost `8.663ms` via blit-used versus `66.720ms` old full-state memcpy. At `pos2048`, eight 27B branches copy `3.245GiB` in `20.276ms` via blit-used. The GPU self-spec pipeline now uses live blit backup/restore by default and has `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-legacy-full-state-backup` for A/B. Reject-prone 9B `noffn_0_2/gen16/schedule=4,4,8` matched parity/acceptance in live and legacy modes (`78.95%`, one reject); backup accounting improved `7.870ms -> 4.621ms`, but end-to-end wall stayed noisy/slower (`844.232ms` legacy vs `860.645ms` live), so this is useful exact state-pool plumbing, not yet a speed breakthrough. Verification: `spec/qwen35_state_copy_spec.cr` now proves a live copy matches a full fork for subsequent exact decode (`2 examples, 0 failures`). Verdict: resident branch-state pool is viable; next tree experiment should implement `k=2` exact tree verifier with preallocated branch slots and pointer/state-slot switch on rescue instead of replay/resync.
- **Post-commit state-pool gate:** rebuilt/spec-tested the resident backup change and ran live-vs-legacy A/B. `spec/qwen35_state_copy_spec.cr` passed (`2 examples, 0 failures`); both probes rebuilt. Branch-copy gate `/tmp/qwen35_branch_state_copy_postcommit_gate.log` measured 27B `pos64/2 branches` at `2.161ms` live blit vs `16.615ms` full-state memcpy, and 27B `pos2048/8 branches` at `20.303ms` live blit vs `67.152ms` full-state memcpy. Real self-spec A/B preserved exact parity and emitted ids on both 9B and 27B. 9B reject route: live `backup_ms=4.654` vs legacy `9.452`, same `78.95%` acceptance / one reject, wall effectively neutral (`862.063ms` vs `859.185ms`). 27B one-chunk route: live `backup_ms=3.025` vs legacy `17.107`, same `100%` acceptance/parity, with lower observed overlap (`493.306ms` vs `558.890ms`) but this is still a single short run. Verdict: the state-pool primitive is correctness-validated and reduces backup cost; it does not by itself solve the self-draft wall.
- **Latest top2-tree prerequisite:** added exact Metal decode-wave top2 support for Q6_K/Q8_0 lm-head tile paths. `forward_top2` and `forward_self_draft_top2` now return `(top1, value1, top2, value2)` without materializing or reading full logits, and token-buffer async self-draft can request top2 while preserving the existing top1 id buffer needed for GPU-resident chaining. Verification: `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` compares Metal top2 against full logits on 9B (`1 example, 0 failures`), the main self-spec probe still builds, and temporary microbench logs show no meaningful top2 head tax (`9B/8 steps`: `top1_ms=185.470`, `top2_ms=183.244`, ratio `0.988`; `27B/4 steps`: `257.060` vs `256.895`, ratio `0.999`). Verdict: real `k=2` tree no longer needs an oracle top2 source; next implementation can branch on real draft top2 buffers and measure branch-state slot switching.
- **Latest scheduler/tree2 audit:** fixed a GPU self-spec schedule bug: the overlapped path submitted `next_steps` from the schedule but read back only `gamma` candidates, so schedule `4,4,8` silently behaved like `4,4,4,...` in the main wall path. After the fix, the 9B stable `noffn_0_2/gen16/schedule=4,4,8` smoke prints `gamma_history=4,4,8`, keeps `parity=true`/`100%` accept, and measures `overlap_ms=630.070` instead of the earlier broken `824.222` reject-shaped run. Added opt-in `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-first`: it requests real draft top2 and early-exits first-token mismatches before verifying a wrong tail. On the stable route it is inert (`checks=3`, `early_exits=0`). On an intentionally hostile `rank8 + draft_skip_recurrent_ffn` route it preserved parity and reduced wrong-tail work (`verifier_tokens 23 -> 7`, `replay_ms 177.474 -> 0`, `overlap_ms 1200.397 -> 664.077`), but all `8` first-token mismatches were top2 misses, so this validates the early-exit machinery, not a production top2-rescue win.
- **Latest long-reasoning tree2 gate:** a 192-token distributed-storage reasoning prompt with `gen=32`, schedule `4,4,8`, layers `0,2,4,6,8,10`, rank48, created the intended miss regime (`greedy_drift_policy top1_match=93.75%`, top5 `100%`). For pure lowrank GPU self-spec, baseline measured `rejections=2`, `accept_rate=78.95%`, `overlap_ms=1854.106`, `plain_speedup=0.4534x`, parity true. Enabling real `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-first` rescued both first-token rejects (`tree2_first_rescues=2`, `misses=0`, `early_exits=2`), reduced verifier tokens `37 -> 31`, removed replay (`53.282 -> 0`), and improved overlap to `1430.232` (`~22.9%` faster than baseline self-spec), still slower than plain exact (`plain_speedup=0.5709x`). The same prompt with aggressive `noffn_prefix4` also improved via early-exit (`3357.782 -> 3127.730`) but had `rescues=0`, proving candidate quality determines whether top2 is a rescue or only a wrong-tail guard. Oracle `top_k=2` on pure lowrank matched this: `top1_rate=93.75%`, `topk_rate=100%`, `misses=0`.
- **Latest tree2-anywhere implementation:** added opt-in `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-anywhere`, an exact serial verifier inside each draft chunk that can inspect real top2 at any mismatch position and stops before wrong-tail verification. It is a correctness/search primitive, not a production scheduler. Smoke on hostile `rank8 + draft_skip_recurrent_ffn` preserved parity and reproduced the expected miss pattern (`checks=8`, `rescues=0`, `misses=8`). On the long reasoning pure-lowrank prompt it rescued the same `2/2` top2 misses as `tree2_first` (`tree2_anywhere_rescues=2`, `misses=0`, parity true), but wall regressed to `overlap_ms=1876.081` because accepted chunks lost chunk-major verifier/overlap; `tree2_first` stayed much better at `1430.232`. Verdict: the next production version should be staged/branch-state tree2, not serial-anywhere: keep chunk-major overlap for accepted chunks, but split or branch only when a guard/margin predicts a likely mismatch.
- **Latest staged tree2 verifier finding:** added opt-in `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-staged=N`, which keeps the normal chunk-major next-draft submission but verifies the current proposal in N-token exact stages, using real Metal top2 at the first stage mismatch and restoring/replaying only the corrected stage prefix. Correctness gates passed (`crystal build`, `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr`, hostile `rank8 + draft_skip_recurrent_ffn` smoke with `parity=true`, `misses=8`). On the long reasoning pure-lowrank prompt it rescued the same `2/2` misses with parity, but is refuted as a speed path in this form: staged=2 measured `overlap_ms=1824.750` (`17` verifier chunks) and staged=4 measured `1858.444` (`10` verifier chunks), both far slower than first-token tree2 `1430.232` and only neutral/slightly better than baseline `1854.106`. Verdict: verifier chunk splitting repeats the FFN-heavy verifier body; the next tree path should use resident branch-state slots / guard-triggered branch verification, not blanket staged verification.
- **Latest owned-resync cleanup:** removed an avoidable second `State#fork` in GPU self-spec reject/resync paths by adding an owned seed submission path for already-exclusive resync states. This is exact and mostly helps reject/tree paths; initial seeds still fork the shared prompt state. Verification: build passed, top2 spec passed, hostile `rank8 + draft_skip_recurrent_ffn + tree2_first` smoke kept `parity=true` with `8` early exits. On the long reasoning pure-lowrank gate, baseline stayed effectively unchanged (`1849.876ms` vs prior `1854.106ms`), while tree2-first improved slightly from `1430.232ms` to `1417.600ms`; `draft_fork_ms` is now about `4.9ms` on the measured rows. Treat this as a narrow resync cleanup, not a breakthrough.
- **Latest live-used resync base copy:** extended the owned-resync path to build resync bases through `copy_state_metal_used!` into a fresh prepared state instead of full `State#fork`, then consume that state directly as the next draft branch. This is the first small resident branch-state primitive in the self-spec scheduler. Verification: build/top2 spec passed; normal reject-heavy smoke and tree2-first reject-heavy smoke both kept `parity=true`. Short reject-heavy normal path improved in same-binary A/B from `1211.727ms` (owned fork base) to `1147.740ms` (live-used copy base); long reasoning tree2-first improved from `1417.600ms` to `1407.845ms`. Remaining wall is still verifier/draft body, not state copy.
- **Latest tree2 margin guard probe:** added opt-in `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-margin-guard=F` plus top1/top2 margin attribution for self-spec draft blocks. The margin signal is useful diagnostically: on the long reasoning pure-lowrank gate, all proposed draft tokens had `tree2_margin_avg=1.2855`, while the two rejected first-token misses had `tree2_reject_margin_avg=0.0527` and `min=0.0024`. The direct guard scheduler is not promoted: guard-only at threshold `0.1` preserved parity but regressed to `overlap_ms=1827.613` with false-pass splits; `tree2_first+guard` preserved parity but remained noise-level versus tree2-first alone (`1428.828ms` vs same-binary `1449.354ms`, previous live-copy tree2-first `1407.845ms`) while adding verifier splits. Verdict: keep the default-off margin probe for finding low-confidence/later-token reject regimes, but do not use simple margin-triggered verifier splitting as the production path. Next speed path remains resident branch-state/top-k verification that avoids repeating FFN-heavy verifier work on accepted chunks.
- **Latest hybrid/updown sweep:** on the same long reasoning gate at `gen=16`, non-rich hybrid sweep with `tree2_first` and updown rank32 refuted FFN pca-updown/no-FFN as the next speed lever for this route. Pure lowrank stayed best (`plain_speedup=0.5597`, `overlap_ms=744.915`, accept `78.95%`). Best hybrid row was still slower (`hybrid_n0_u10`: `plain_speedup=0.5143`, `842.127ms`), and all-layer updown-32 was much worse (`plain_speedup=0.2776`, accept `43.48%`, `6` rejects). Keep updown/hybrid default-off; only reopen if a larger prompt suite identifies a route with higher accept and lower draft wait, not just lower per-layer math.
- **Latest wasted-draft attribution:** added `attr_draft_resyncs`, `attr_draft_wasted_tail_tokens`, and `attr_draft_wasted_next_tokens` to quantify the upper bound for branch-state/top-k scheduling. On the long reasoning `gen=32` gate, baseline pure self-spec wasted `6` current-tail tokens plus `11` already-submitted next-block tokens over `2` resyncs (`overlap_ms=1871.182`). `tree2_first` kept the same `2` resyncs and `6` tail tokens but reduced wasted next-block tokens to `0`, verifier tokens `37 -> 31`, and wall to `1409.135`. This means branch-state can still target resync cost, but wrong-tail/next-block waste is not large enough alone; the larger walls are draft body and verifier body/serialization.
- **Latest suite route-stability tooling:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-suite-hybrid-sweep`, which applies the existing hybrid route sweep to `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-suite-prompt` prompts and prints both per-row `self_spec_route_scoreboard` and aggregate `self_spec_route_stability_scoreboard`. The aggregate groups by mode/split/route/updown, hard-gates parity, reports prompt count, baseline count, mean/min baseline delta, mean accept/plain speed, total overlap, and max replay. Verification: `/tmp/qwen35_suite_hybrid_probe` build passed; a 9B `layers=0,2`, rank8, `gamma=2`, `gen=2`, main+code suite smoke emitted `self_spec_route_stability_scoreboard groups=3 baselines=2` with parity true on all rows; `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` still passes.
- **Latest short-suite vs long-reasoning route update:** short 9B main/code/json `gen=16`, `tree2_first`, `layers=0,2,4,6,8,10`, rank48 showed `noffn_0_2` slightly faster than pure on all three rows (`overlap_ms`: main `613.681 -> 593.527`, code `1090.295 -> 986.981`, JSON `898.668 -> 877.142`), but the older long-reasoning hybrid gate still refutes `noffn_0_2` as a global default (`744.915ms` pure vs `1069.928ms` noffn_0_2 at `gen=16`). Treat no-FFN as prompt/route-dependent; require the new stability scoreboard before considering any fusion/default change.
- **Latest long-reasoning gamma falsifier:** on the 192-token reasoning prompt with pure lowrank, `tree2_first`, `gen=32`, rank48, and same-run schedule sweep, `4,4,8` remained best (`overlap_ms=1518.206`, `plain_speedup=0.5509x`, parity true). Larger schedules reduced chunk count but increased waste/draft wait and regressed: `4,8,16` `1858.518ms`, `8,8` `1834.366ms`, `8,16` `1865.753ms`, all parity true. Do not chase larger gamma on this route until a branch-state/guard path can avoid long wrong tails and pre-submitted wrong next blocks.
- **Latest route-order + suite sweep:** route sweeps now emit pure baseline before manual candidates to reduce order bias in in-process perf scoreboards. With the corrected order, focused 9B main/code/json `gen=16` suite ranked `noffn_0_2` first (`mean baseline_delta=+7.49%`, `min=+2.93%`, parity all true), `noffn_suffix4/manual=4,6,8,10` second (`mean=+6.56%`, `min=+4.05%`), and pure baseline last. Earlier 9B rich `gen=8` suite ranked `noffn_suffix4` first (`mean=+4.28%`, `min=+2.80%`). This is a useful route-map signal for default/code/json, but not a direct exact-decode win (`plain_speedup` stays below `1.0`) and still needs the long-reasoning falsifier before promotion.
- **Latest long-reasoning route refutation:** the focused long-reasoning `gen=16` gate refuted the default/code/json no-FFN winners as global routes. Pure lowrank stayed best (`overlap_ms=735.740`, `accept=78.95%`, `plain_speedup=0.5543x`), while `noffn_0` regressed to `931.148ms`, `noffn_0_2` to `1038.631ms`, and `noffn_suffix4/manual=4,6,8,10` collapsed to `1372.102ms` with `43.48%` acceptance. Conclusion: no-FFN route maps need prompt/risk-aware selection; do not promote any of these maps as default.
- **Latest prompt route-risk instrumentation:** added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-route-features`, auto-enabled by `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-suite-hybrid-sweep`, to print held-out PCA residual summaries per prompt/layer set (`self_spec_prompt_route_features`). The feature-only path now also works for suite prompts without running speculative decode. On a 9B rank48/layers `0,2,4,6,8,10` feature suite, default/code/json prompts clustered around residual mean `0.154-0.161` and pass@0.2 `64.57-65.74%`, while the reasoning prompt separated at residual mean `0.258263` and pass@0.2 `34.72%`. This is a useful candidate prompt-risk signal for no-FFN route selection, not yet a policy. Verification: `/tmp/qwen35_route_features_probe2` build passed; `/tmp/qwen35_route_features_feature_only_suite_smoke.log` emitted suite features without pipeline flags; `/tmp/qwen35_route_features_9b_prompt_suite_rank48_feature_only.log` captured the four-prompt feature split; `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` passed with bridge link flags.
- **Latest route-risk falsifier:** aggregate residual features are not sufficient as a route policy. A same-prompt 9B `gen=16` route-vs-feature gate kept parity on all rows, but the high-residual reasoning prompt still preferred `noffn_0_2` over pure (`baseline_delta=+3.49%`), while the low-residual code prompt rejected the manual suffix route (`accept=83.33%`, `replay_ms=45.06`, `baseline_delta=-39.80%`). Added per-layer feature lines (`self_spec_prompt_route_layer_features`) to make route maps diagnosable. Per-layer residuals highlight suffix-layer risk on code (`layer10 mean=0.314`), but they still are only a diagnostic: main/json tolerate similar suffix residuals, and reasoning tolerates high early-layer residuals. Next classifier must combine residuals with route-specific draft quality/margin/acceptance probes, not hard-code residual thresholds.
- **Latest route-scoreboard risk fields:** route scoreboards now include `margin_min` and `reject_margin_min`, sourced from the already-collected draft top1/top2 margins. This keeps low-margin reject risk visible in the ranking table without rerunning full logs. Smoke verification shows the new columns in both per-row and stability scoreboards. In the gen16 route-vs-feature gate, the rejected code/manual suffix row had `tree2_reject_margin_min=0.0131`; stable rows generally had much larger route margins. Do not use this alone as a splitter yet: earlier simple margin-guard verifier splitting regressed, so margin is a route-risk diagnostic until combined with a cheaper branch-state/top-k verifier.
- **Latest block-surrogate self-spec gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-self-spec-gammas=LIST`, a CPU exact self-spec acceptance gate for block residual draft proposals. It drafts with the trained block surrogate, verifies against an exact greedy baseline, and reports `parity`, `verifier_parity`, accept/reject counts, draft top2/top5 hit rates, chunk histories, and exact/emitted/baseline ids. Verification: release-built `/tmp/qwen35_block_selfspec_probe`; `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` passed. On 9B default prompt with `tokens=32`, `calib=16`, `rank=16`, `gen=8`, state mode `skip`, late layers `25:25` and `28:28` kept `100%` accept and `parity=true` for `gamma=2,4,8`; `24:24` preserved parity but had one reject (`77.78%` accept) for `gamma=2,4`. Decision: `25:25` and `28:28` are the next block-surrogate candidates for larger prompt-suite and eventual GPU-resident draft-body experiments; `24:24` is useful as a reject/control case, not a promotion candidate yet.
- **Latest late-block surrogate suite:** first adversarial prompt-class screen supports the "late layers mostly refine token choice" hypothesis, but with margin caveats. On 9B `tokens=32/calib=16/rank16/gen8/gamma8`, blocks `25:25`, `28:28`, and `24:24` all kept `100%` accept/parity on code and JSON prompts. On the reasoning prompt, `28:28` still kept `100%` accept/parity, while `25:25` had one reject (`77.78%` accept) and `24:24` had two rejects (`50%` accept); all rejected rows still had `parity=true` because exact verification corrected them. Stressing `28:28` on reasoning to `gen16/gamma8,16` produced one reject (`88.24%` accept), with the exact token still in draft top5. A longer prompt boundary (`tokens=48`) changed the generated segment and passed `100%` accept/parity even at `calib16/rank16/gamma16`, so do not over-attribute that improvement to rank/calibration. Decision: `28:28` is currently the strongest late-single candidate; `25:25` remains promising; `24:24` is prompt-margin-sensitive. Next gate should add prompt-suite support in-process and report wall-cost/microbench before any default policy.
- **Latest block-surrogate suite tooling:** added one-process block-surrogate sweep support: `--simulate-block-surrogate-suite-blocks=LIST` plus `--simulate-block-surrogate-suite-prompt=NAME::TEXT` / `--simulate-block-surrogate-suite-prompts-file=PATH`. It includes the main prompt automatically, trains each requested block per prompt, runs all `--simulate-block-surrogate-self-spec-gammas`, and prints both `block_surrogate_suite_scoreboard` rows and `block_surrogate_suite_aggregate` grouped by block/mode/rank/gamma with parity, verifier parity, accept mean/min, rejects, top-k hit minima, draft margin minima, hidden cosine, and RMSE. Verification: `/tmp/qwen35_block_suite_probe` build passed; smoke `blocks=25:25,28:28`, main+code, `rank12/gen4/gamma4` emitted row and aggregate scoreboards with `parity=true`; `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` passed. Next: run the real 9B/27B late-block suite with `24-30`, `rank16/24`, `gen8/16`, and default/code/json/reasoning prompts.
- **Latest real late-block surrogate suite:** ran the real 9B one-process suite with default/code/json/reasoning prompts. Single-layer screen `tokens=32/calib16/rank16/gen8/gamma8`, blocks `24-30`, kept `parity_all=true`; blocks `25:25`, `26:26`, and `27:27` were clean across all four prompts (`accept_mean=min=100%`, `0` rejects), `28:28` was near-clean (`96.88%` mean, `87.5%` min, one reject), while `24:24`, `29:29`, and `30:30` were prompt-margin-sensitive. Band screen at the same shape showed `26:27`, `25:27`, and `25:26` all preserving parity with `96.88%` mean / `87.5%` min accept; `25:28` was refuted as too wide at rank16 (`78.46%` mean, `53.85%` min, `3` rejects, top2/top5 min `87.5%`). Longer stress `tokens=40/calib16/gen16` promoted `26:27/gamma8` as the best current band candidate (`92.89%` mean, `83.33%` min, `2` rejects, top5 min `100%`, parity all true); `gamma16` regressed (`77.14%` mean, `60%` min), and `25:27` was weaker. Decision: next implementation/cost gate should target `26:27` with short/progressive chunks, keep `25:25/26:26/27:27` as single-layer controls, and avoid `25:28` or fixed `gamma16` at rank16 until a stronger adapter/risk gate exists. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_suite_real_20260430_065728.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_suite_bands_20260430_073556.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_suite_bands_stress_20260430_075807.log`.
- **Latest block-surrogate wall-cost gate:** suite rows now print decode timing attribution: `baseline_decode_ms`, `draft_ms`, `draft_fork_ms`, `verifier_ms`, `verifier_fork_ms`, sequential self-spec decode time, and an optimistic `ideal_overlap_speedup`. Focused 9B `26:27`, `tokens=40/calib16/rank16/gen16/gamma8`, default/code/json/reasoning preserved exact parity and reproduced the acceptance aggregate (`92.89%` mean, `83.33%` min), but refuted immediate speed promotion: mean exact baseline decode was `10727.5ms`, draft was `11737.8ms`, verifier was `11238.0ms`, sequential self-spec was `23565.8ms` (`0.455x`), and even ideal overlap was only `0.876x`. Interpretation: replacing two late layers is acceptance-promising but not enough work removal; the next speed path must either make the surrogate body much cheaper/fused, replace a wider band with a stronger/risk-gated adapter, or exploit the existing fast chunk verifier path rather than this CPU probe route. Verification: `/tmp/qwen35_block_wall_probe` build passed, `/tmp/qwen35_block_wall_26_27_20260430_121058.log`, `spec/qwen35_decode_top2_spec.cr` passed.
- **Latest static block-surrogate drift refutation:** added probe-only `--block-surrogate-oracle-gen-calib=N`, which adds N exact generated-token block samples to adapter training while still drafting from the original prompt boundary. The wider `20:27/rank48` route was promising only as a short-horizon signal: at `gen8/gamma4`, main+reason had `100%` accept and draft was cheaper than baseline (`~4310ms` vs `~4967ms`), but `gen16` refuted it as a steady route (`gamma2` aggregate `70.69%` mean / `52.63%` min, `14` rejects; `gamma4` `57.75%` mean / `34.62%` min). `25:28/rank32/gen16` was safer but still not stable enough (`88.76%` mean / `78.95%` min, `4` rejects, ideal overlap `0.945x`). Mixture adapters did not help (`20:27` global `92.59%` mean on main/code/reason versus mixture `85.19%`; `20:30` stayed bad). Oracle generated calibration did not fix drift (`20:27/rank48/gen16/oracle8/gamma2` `69.12%` mean / `52.63%` min), so naive rolling/online adapter refresh is not sufficient. Decision: do not build a superfused kernel for the current static block-residual surrogate. Next speed search should pivot away from static block residual maps toward a learned/route-gated proposal head, exact tree/top-k branch reuse for existing lowrank routes, or another candidate source whose gen16 acceptance remains stable. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_wall_20_27_rank48_gen16_20260430_145207.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_wall_25_28_rank32_gen16_20260430_151230.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_wall_mixture_rank48_20260430_142435.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_oracle_20_27_rank48_gen16_20260430_152656.log`.
- **Latest block-surrogate top-K/tree oracle gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-tree-oracle=K`, which scores static block-surrogate proposal distributions as a top-K branch upper bound and prints `block_surrogate_tree_oracle` / `block_surrogate_tree_aggregate` rows. The broad `20:27/rank48` candidate is refuted even with flexibility on main/gen16: top-2 only covers `56.25%` and leaves `7` misses. The safer `25:28/rank32` candidate shows why the branch remains worth studying, but not yet worth fusing: top1 self-spec accept is weak (`45.83%`), while top-5 covers `93.75%` with one miss and average rank-ordered branch cost `1.75`; rank48 and oracle generated calibration did not remove the miss (`93.75%` top-5, one miss). Interpretation: static block residual has useful candidate mass, but a production win needs a better reranker/learned candidate source or a wider stable surrogate; simply fusing the current map would optimize a route that still needs too much verifier/branch work. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_rank48_k2_gen16_20260430_182527.log` (interrupted after `20:27` refute), `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_main_gen16_20260430_183337.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank48_k5_main_gen16_20260430_183931.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_oracle8_suite_main_gen16_20260430_184710.log`.
- **Latest block-surrogate warmup/reranker gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-topk-oracle=K`, `--simulate-block-surrogate-topk-oracle-train-tokens=N`, and `--simulate-block-surrogate-tree-warmup=N`. On main/gen16, `25:28/rank32` shows a concrete controller shape: raw top-5 has one cold-start miss, but exact warmup of one generated token makes the remaining 15 drafted tokens top-5 clean (`topK=100%`, `misses=0`, avg rank branch `1.733`); warmup2/4/8 are also clean with avg rank branch `1.571/1.583/1.5`. The simple token/rank-bias reranker did not improve held-out branch cost (`top5 train8`: held-out already `100%`, avg branch `1.5`; `K=8/16` did not recover the cold miss in the train half). Code prompt adversary refutes global promotion: with warmup1, top-5 has `86.67%` coverage and `2` misses; top-16 still has `93.33%` and `1` miss, avg branch `2.467`. Decision: one-token exact warmup + static block tree is a real candidate for some prompt modes, but needs a prompt/risk router or additional candidate source before Metal fusion. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_topk_25_28_rank32_k5_train8_main_gen16_20260430_190247.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_warmup1_main_gen16_20260430_193412.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_warmup2_main_gen16_20260430_192833.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_warmup4_main_gen16_20260430_192242.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_warmup1_code_gen16_20260430_193958.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k16_warmup1_code_gen16_20260430_194546.log`.
- **Latest prefill-info falsifier:** tested whether the warmup win is just missing prompt/prefill information. Training `25:28/rank32` on almost the full prompt (`calib=95` out of `96`) improves held-out hidden cosine (`0.9347`) but does **not** remove the main no-warmup top-5 miss (`93.75%`, `1` miss), so more prompt samples alone are insufficient. The useful interpretation is exact prefill seed: the first generated token is already available from final prompt logits, so starting block-tree after that token can be close to free in a real generator. On code, `state_mode=shadow` with warmup1 gives the same top-5 failures as skip mode (`86.67%`, `2` misses), so code failures are not from stale block state; they are candidate-distribution/output-map failures. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_calib95_main_gen16_20260430_204528.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_warmup1_shadow_code_gen16_20260430_205130.log`.
- **Latest explicit prefill-seed gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-prefill-seed` and tree metrics `prefill_seed`, `prefill_seed_tokens`, `tree_tokens`, `avg_*_branch_tokens_total` so the probe distinguishes final-prompt-logit token selection from the exact state advance needed for token 2. Build/spec passed; the no-flag smoke still reports `prefill_seed=false`. Main prompt reproduces the warmup1 win as a scheduler boundary (`prefill_seed=true`, `prefill_seed_tokens=1`, `tree_tokens=15`, `topK=100%`, `misses=0`, avg rank branch `1.733`, total avg rank pressure `1.625`). Code prompt remains refuted (`topK=86.67%`, `2` misses), so this is a cost-boundary improvement, not a global static-block promotion. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_prefill_seed_main_gen16_20260430_211020.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_prefill_seed_code_gen16_20260430_211559.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank16_k5_no_prefill_seed_smoke_20260430_212137.log`.
- **Latest real branch-verify gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-tree-branch-verify`, which actually forks exact pre-token state and advances top-K candidates rank-order for non-final generated tokens instead of only scoring oracle coverage. Main `25:28/rank32/top5/gen16/prefill_seed` remains exact and clean (`topK=100%`, `misses=0`), but costs `25` exact branch attempts for `15` tree tokens (`11` wasted, `18.37s` CPU branch verifier time). Code remains negative (`topK=86.67%`, `2` misses/corrections, `25` attempts, `17.88s`). This refutes immediate static block-tree superfusion: we need fewer attempts via reranking/risk routing/lower K or a cheap GPU branch-state verifier before this can beat plain decode. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_branch_verify_main_gen16_20260501_065642.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_branch_verify_code_gen16_20260501_070238.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank16_k5_branch_verify_off_smoke_20260501_070824.log`.
- **Latest select-advance lower-bound gate:** added `--simulate-block-surrogate-tree-select-advance`, mutually exclusive with rank-order branch verification. It advances only the exact selected token branch from the exact pre-token state, so it removes wasted top-K attempts and measures the lower bound for a future mismatch-only top2 rescue. Main `25:28/rank32/gen16/prefill_seed` at `K=2` has `topK=80.0%`, `3` corrections, `14` attempts, `10.01s` branch time; `K=5` has `topK=100%`, zero corrections, `14` attempts, `10.58s` branch time. Code at `K=2` remains imperfect (`topK=86.67%`, `2` corrections, `10.63s`). Verdict: selecting the right branch fixes wasted rank-order work (`25 -> 14` attempts on main K5) but still pays one full exact state advance per non-final tree token; production value requires mismatch-only branch reuse on top of a chunk verifier or a GPU-resident branch-state verifier, not per-token select-advance as a standalone path. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank16_k5_select_advance_off_smoke_20260501_095110.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k2_select_advance_main_gen16_20260501_095312.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k5_select_advance_main_gen16_20260501_095851.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_tree_25_28_rank32_k2_select_advance_code_gen16_20260501_100438.log`.
- **Latest adaptive-filter falsifier:** added `--block-surrogate-error-feedback=LIST`, a one-token-lag EWMA residual-error correction probe inspired by control theory/adaptive filters. It warms the residual-error bias on calibration samples, then applies the previous exact-observed block residual error to the next surrogate prediction. This is a hidden-residual quality probe, not a speed claim. On `25:28/rank32/tokens96/calib48`, fast feedback is harmful (`decay=0` regresses rel RMSE by `20-30%`), while slow feedback is only narrow-positive: main improves rel RMSE by `6.23%` at `decay=0.95`, code by `2.39%`. Verdict: static block-surrogate error is not a simple low-frequency bias we can cheaply cancel; keep this as diagnostic only. Next adjacent-science candidates with better odds: compressive-sensing FFN sparsity with real sparse-down cost, decision-theoretic mismatch-only top2 rescue, and coding-theory/list-decoding candidate fusion. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_block_feedback_25_28_rank32_main_20260501_101928.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_block_feedback_25_28_rank32_code_20260501_102103.log`.
- **Latest FFN block-sparsity gate:** added `--simulate-ffn-block-sparsity=LIST` plus `lowrank-ffn-blocktop-P`. The 256-channel block energy probe refutes a naive Q4_K block-skip sparse-down kernel: main prompt over layers `0,2,4,6,8,10,24,25,26,28,29,30` needs `read95_mean=87.55%` of blocks, and the code prompt needs `87.25%`; even a hypothetical 64-channel prepack still needs `82.14%`. However the verifier-quality gate is positive: `lowrank-ffn-blocktop-10/20/40` kept `100%` accept/parity for `layers=0,2,4`, `rank64`, `gen8` on default and code prompts, and `blocktop-10/20` also kept `100%` on the late bucket `24,25,26,28,29,30`. Verdict: direct energy-preserving sparse-down is refuted, but approximate block-top FFN remains alive as a candidate-source/sparse-kernel branch; next gate should run a larger prompt suite and only then consider a true block-sparse `ffn_down` kernel. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_block_sparsity_main_20260501.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_block_sparsity_code_20260501.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_block_sparsity_main_b64_20260501.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_blocktop_accept_024_20260501.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_blocktop_accept_024_code_20260501.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_blocktop_accept_late_20260501.log`.
- **Latest decision-boundary sparse-draft synthesis:** persisted `LM-QWEN35-DECISION-BOUNDARY-SPARSE-DRAFT-1` and cfmem decision `d968d995-3cb1-45c3-975b-ec975d140d27`. Working frame: same-weight draft should optimize exact-verifier acceptance/topK, not hidden/energy preservation. The real FFN speed path is a block selector from `ffn_in` before dense `gate/up`, then selected-block `gate/up -> SwiGLU -> down`; current `blocktop` is only a dense quality oracle. Next falsifiers: broader `blocktop-10/20` suite, block-selector oracle/predictor, decision-boundary block scoring, and mismatch-only top2 rescue from verifier mismatch state.
- **Latest blocktop suite gate:** broader 9B `gen16`, `layers=0,2,4`, `rank64`, schedule `4` suite over default/code/json/reasoning prompts keeps the decision-boundary FFN branch alive but exposes replay risk. Baseline lowrank kept `100%` accept/parity on all 4 prompts. `blocktop-20` and `blocktop-10` kept exact parity on all prompts; code/json/reasoning stayed `100%` accept, while default had one correction (`93.75%` accept) for both `blocktop-20` and `blocktop-10`. Current timings are dense-probe noise and not speed evidence; use the result as a quality gate only. Next: add `ffn_in -> top-block` selector/predictor and score selected blocks by acceptance/topK, not energy. Log: `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_blocktop_suite_024_gen16_20260501.log`.
- **Latest FFN block-selector probe:** added `--simulate-ffn-block-selector=LIST` and `--ffn-block-selector-percents=LIST`, a lightweight nearest-neighbor predictability gate that chooses top FFN activation blocks from calibration `ffn_in` neighbors before dense `gate/up`. On 9B `tokens96/calib48`, early layers are promising: default prompt `0/2` at `top10` retained `96.64%/94.09%` of oracle top-block energy, code retained `98.99%/97.56%`; layer `4` is weaker (`66-76%`). Late bucket is mostly weak except layer `26` (`top10 ratio 76.83%`), with layers `24/28/30` around `41-48%`. Verdict: block selection is layer-specific. Next acceptance probe should target predicted-block variants for layers `0,2` first, not uniform all-layer sparse FFN. - **Active self-draft breakthrough plan (persisted for context compaction):**
  1. Use the automatic route scoreboards for rich self-draft sweeps: hard-gate `parity=true`, rank primarily by same-run pure-lowrank `overlap_ms` improvement when available, and require aggregate prompt stability before any route promotion.
  2. Run suite-hybrid sweeps on real targets: 9B quick (`layers=0,2,4,6,8,10`, `gen=8/16`), 27B main (`layers=24,25,26,28,29,30`, `gen=16`, schedule `4,4,8`), ranks `48/64`, updown ranks `16/32`, and at least default/code/json/reasoning prompt classes.
  3. Pick one stable layer-mode map before writing more Metal: promote only if parity holds, acceptance is not worse than baseline unless replay cost is clearly compensated, `overlap_ms` improves by about `>=10%` on at least two prompt classes, and `plain_speedup` moves upward without replay spikes.
  4. Super-fuse only the winning GPU-resident draft route: keep `norm -> lowrank/DN -> selected FFN mode -> residual` inside the draft lane, avoid host branching/readback, and do not optimize LM-head/readback first because state-only/no-head already refuted that as the main wall.
  5. Test math tricks after the map is stable: residual-delta PCA (`ffn_out - ffn_in`), per-layer rank selection, shared PCA bases for neighboring stable layers, confidence-gated updown, and n-gram only as a skip-router for obvious low-entropy/repeat spans.
  6. Add a state-resident exact tree verifier branch: `k=2` first, use real Metal draft top2 plus `copy_state_metal_used!(..., used_tokens: current_pos)` into preallocated branch slots, verify rank-ordered branches before generating a long wrong tail, and report replay/resync tax avoided versus added branch-copy/verifier cost. Do not pursue blanket staged verifier splitting unless a guard predicts likely mismatch; staged=2/4 repeated too much verifier work on the long reasoning gate.
  7. Continue two non-refuted branches from the block-surrogate tree gate: (a) top-K/tree branch reuse with exact warmup/risk routing and real branch-state verifier; (b) a better candidate source/reranker that can cover code-like misses where static `25:28` top-16 still fails. Do not super-fuse static block residual until a multi-prompt `gen16+` route has near-clean top-K coverage and an estimated cost below plain exact decode.
  8. Test decision-boundary sparse FFN before writing kernels: first broaden `lowrank-ffn-blocktop-10/20` across default/code/json/reasoning prompts and `gen16+`; then add an `ffn_in -> top-blocks` oracle/predictor. Promote only if acceptance/topK stays high and estimated selected-block traffic is low enough to beat dense draft body.
- **Current accelerator portfolio after 2026-05-05 Quadrumvirate (persisted for context compaction):**
  1. **Cleaner same-weight/self-draft chunks** are the highest-priority decode path. Scalar MTP staging/off-ramp controllers are exhausted; speed requires better proposal quality or cheaper resident verification.
  2. **Projected-K low-rank DeltaNet as an internal draft branch** remains stronger than exact target replacement. Exact verifier turns low-rank drift into rejects; route it by layer/head/prompt risk instead of trusting residual norm alone.
  3. **DN physical/regime diagnostics** are the next cheap test: inspect `g`, `beta`, effective decay horizon `tau=-1/log(g)`, K residual, and approximate eligibility by layer/head. Hypothesis: fast-forgetting heads/layers can tolerate more aggressive low-rank DN, while long-memory regimes need exact or lower gamma.
  4. **Exact associative DN scan** stays a long-prefill (`pp1024+`) research branch only. Existing affine/compact algebra is exact, but current compact-summary and adjoint paths lose to `delta_net_chunk_128_rowwise`; revive only with rank-stable/fused composition or a new formulation.
  5. **LTP/WBA should be used as glue**, not another scalar knob: resident verifier state handoff, branch-state logs/copies, summary construction, and two-lane draft/verifier overlap.
  6. **Prompt/session cache** is an exact product accelerator: repeated prompts/prefixes should restore KV + DeltaNet recurrent state by hash/session instead of recomputing.

- **Qwen3.6 MTP sidecar branch:**
  - [x] Verify official HF Qwen3.6 MTP tensors exist and local LM Studio GGUF dropped them.
  - [x] Add range extractor `scripts/qwen36_extract_mtp_sidecar.py`; it repacks only `mtp.*` byte ranges into `/Users/sergey/.cache/cogni-ml/qwen36_mtp/Qwen3.6-27B-mtp.safetensors` (`15` BF16 tensors, `810.05 MiB`) instead of downloading full `4.5 GiB` shards.
  - [x] Add minimal Crystal safetensors reader and `Qwen35MTPWeights` loader plus `bin/qwen35_mtp_sidecar_probe.cr`; probe validates sidecar shapes against local 27B GGUF hparams.
  - [x] Implement first CPU/BF16 MTP forward oracle (`num_speculative_tokens=1`) using sidecar BF16 weights and shared target embedding/lm-head. Critical semantic fix: raw HF Qwen3.5/Qwen3.6 MTP RMSNorm tensors must be applied as `(1 + weight)`. First 27B smokes: `The capital of France is` accepts predicted token 2 (`"."`), `def fibonacci(n):` accepts indentation token, and `Once upon a time` rejects top1 (`" in"` vs exact `" there"`) but exact token is MTP top2/top5. CPU oracle is intentionally slow (`~10s` MTP step), so this is formula/quality evidence, not speed evidence.
  - [x] Add the exact one-token MTP shortcut: for `num_speculative_tokens=1`, MTP attention has only the current MTP token in its cache, so softmax is exactly `1` and Q/K/RoPE cannot affect the output. The oracle now computes only Q-proj gate rows plus V/O/FFN/head by default and keeps `QWEN35_MTP_ONE_TOKEN_SHORTCUT_OFF=1` for A/B. Logical BF16 MTP body traffic drops from `810 MiB` to `740 MiB`; CPU oracle A/B was exact and only small/noisy (`~9.50s` shortcut vs `~9.63s` full on one France run), so the main value is a Metal/quant design constraint rather than a CPU win.
  - [x] Add first Metal BF16 MTP GEMV path (`-D qwen35_mtp_metal`): generic row-major BF16 GEMV plus q-proj gate-row GEMV, cached sidecar weight upload, and CPU fallback via `QWEN35_MTP_BF16_METAL_OFF=1`. Focused tiny Metal check passed, MTP specs still pass without Metal link, and 27B France smoke preserved acceptance/top5 while reducing MTP body wall from `9450.892 ms` CPU BF16 to `157.253 ms` Metal BF16 in a fresh process. This is still per-matvec command-buffer/readback infrastructure, not yet a fused resident MTP body.
  - [x] Correct the MTP timing interpretation: fresh-process `mtp_metal_bf16` includes one-time sidecar weight uploads into cached MetalBuffers. In-process warm repeat on 27B France top1 measured `173.768 ms -> 11.229 ms -> 10.166 ms` with identical token/logit. This makes MTP a serious candidate source after warmup; next benchmarks must separate cold upload, warm body, and end-to-end speculative decode.
  - [x] Add greedy MTP head fast path for already-normalized MTP hidden: `Qwen35Metal.project_top1_no_norm` reuses the Q6/Q8 top1 tile reducers without reading full logits, and `bin/qwen35_mtp_sidecar_probe.cr --top1-only` exercises it. France top1 smoke preserved acceptance (`"."`) and measured `mtp_metal_bf16_top1=159.473 ms`; full-logits/top5 in a nearby fresh process measured `195.138 ms`. Story top1 preserved the known reject (`" in"` vs exact `" there"`). This confirms full-logit readback is removable for greedy proposals, but the remaining wall is still mostly the MTP body, not the head.
  - [x] Add opt-in one-command resident MTP body prototype (`QWEN35_MTP_BODY_METAL=1`): fc/RMSNorm/V/qgate/attention gate/O/addnorm/FFN/final norm stay GPU-resident and return only final hidden. Correctness smoke preserved France acceptance, but fresh-process A/B was neutral/noisy (`170.937 ms` default per-matvec top1 vs `178.042 ms` resident body in one paired run; an earlier run was slower). Keep default off and treat this as a falsifier: simply coalescing dispatches into one command buffer is not enough; the next resident version must fuse larger kernels or reduce BF16 body bytes.
  - [x] Extend `bin/qwen35_mtp_sidecar_probe.cr` with official in-process MTP warmup/repeats and multi-prompt first-step suite rows. 27B warm top1-only rows after one warmup are usually `~10-11 ms` per MTP proposal (`france=10.656 ms`, `code=10.151 ms`, `story=10.492 ms`, JSON retry `10.152 ms`), while a full-logits/top5 4-prompt suite shows top1 acceptance `2/4` and exact token in MTP top5 `4/4`. This confirms topK/tree rescue is not optional for broad prompts.

On the 4-prompt 27B/top5/gen8 suite, teacher-forced MTP over exact target hiddens gets `20/32` top1 and `29/32` top5 hits, but recursive MTP chaining collapses to `3/32` top1 and `8/32` top5 hits. This refutes plain recursive MTP gamma>1 as the next speed path; the useful signal is exact-hidden/topK candidate quality.

  - [x] Falsifier: prompt-local diagonal hidden bridge is unstable. `--mtp-chain-diag-bridge` trains a diagonal `raw_mtp_hidden -> target_hidden` map from prompt transitions, but on the 4-prompt 27B/top5/gen8 suite it regresses reliability (`recursive_diag` totals `1/32` top1 and `8/32` top5) and adds expensive calibration (`~0.5-2.0s` in the short prompts).

On the 4-prompt 27B teacher-forced `gen8` suite, `K=2` gives `26/32` topK hits with `44` rank-order attempts (`1.375/token`), `K=5` gives `29/32` with `57` attempts (`1.781/token`), and `K=8` only improves to `30/32` while raising pressure to `65` attempts (`2.031/token`). Decision: do not build a blind rank-order K8 verifier; prioritize low-K mismatch-only rescue, branch selection/routing, or an exact-hidden pipeline that avoids pre-advancing branches.

On the 4-prompt 27B teacher-forced `gen8/K=5` suite, exact hidden oracle cost was `2784.512 ms`, MTP cost was `534.022 ms`, serial exact+MTP would be `1.192x` slower than exact alone, and perfect overlap is only neutral (`ideal_overlap_vs_exact=1.0`). Verdict: one-step MTP is a candidate/ranker, not a standalone speedup, unless another route uses it to avoid replay/branch work.

On the same 4-prompt 27B teacher-forced `gen8/K=5` suite, top1 hits have higher average margin (`3.700`) than misses (`0.987`), but the distributions overlap badly (`hit_margin_min=0.580`, `miss_margin_max=5.241`). Verdict: raw MTP margin alone is not a safe router; next confidence gates need richer features such as candidate agreement, prompt class, rank history, or a calibrated classifier.

On the 4-prompt 27B teacher-forced `gen8/K=5` suite, thresholds `0.5/1/2/4` all still selected false top1 candidates (`5/3/2/1` false accepts). A very conservative threshold `5.5` was clean (`5/5`, `0` false accepts) but only selected `15.62%` of tokens and captured `25%` of true top1 hits. Margin thresholds `0.5/1/2/4` still have false accepts (`13/7/3/2`); threshold `5.5` stays clean (`9/9`) but selects only `14.06%` of tokens and captures `22.5%` of true top1 hits.

Short 3-prompt `gen16` suite: `gamma=8` emitted `48` tokens in `16` passes (`3.0 tokens/pass`) with additive model `2.167x`. Longer 3-prompt `gen64` suite: `gamma=8` emitted `192` tokens in `78` passes (`2.462 tokens/pass`) with additive model `1.872x`; `gamma=16` plateaued at the same pass count.
